\{\lambda_{i}\}_{i\in\mathbb{I}}
as the operator
\mathcal{R}
and the eigenfunctions

\displaystyle\phi_{i}(t)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\lambda_{i}}}~\big(\mathcal{Y}^{\star}\psi_{i}\big)({\bm{\mu}})=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\lambda_{i}}}~{\langle\psi_{i},y({\bm{\mu}})\rangle}_{V}

for
i\in\{j\in\mathbb{N}:\lambda_{j}>0\}
and almost all
{\bm{\mu}}\in\mathscr{D}
.
\blacksquare

In the following theorem we formulate properties of the eigenvalues and eigenfunctions of
\mathcal{R}
. Its proof follows from Theorem
2.2.7
.

Theorem 3.7.4
.

Let
\{\lambda_{i}\}_{i\in\mathbb{I}}
and
\{\psi_{i}\}_{i\in\mathbb{I}}
denote the eigenvalues and eigenfunctions, respectively, of the operator
\mathcal{R}
introduced in (
3.7.4
). Then for every
\ell\in\mathbb{I}
the first
\ell
eigenfunctions
\psi_{1},\ldots,\psi_{\ell}\in X
solve the minimization problem

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\min\int_{\mathscr{D}}\Big\|y(\mu)-\sum_{i=1}^{\ell}{\langle y({\bm{\mu}}),\psi_{i}\rangle}_{X}\,\psi_{i}\Big\|_{X}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{\mu}}\\
&\text{s.t. }\{\psi_{i}\}_{i=1}^{\ell}\subset X\text{ and }{\langle\psi_{j},\psi_{i}\rangle}_{X}=\delta_{ij}\text{ for }1\leq i,j\leq\ell.\end{aligned}\right.

(3.7.6)

Moreover,

\int_{\mathscr{D}}\Big\|y({\bm{\mu}})-\sum_{i=1}^{\ell}{\langle y({\bm{\mu}}),\psi_{i}\rangle}_{X}\,\psi_{i}\Big\|_{X}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{\mu}}=\sum_{i>\ell}\lambda_{i}\quad\text{for any }\ell\in\mathbb{N}.

(3.7.7)

We call a solution to (
3.7.6
) a
POD basis of rank
\ell
. Recall that
\{\psi\}_{i=1}^{\ell}
is also the solution to

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &\min-\int_{\mathscr{D}}\sum_{i=1}^{\ell}\big|{\langle y({\bm{\mu}}),\psi_{i}\rangle}_{X}\big|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{\mu}}\\
&\hskip 2.27621pt\text{s.t. }\{\psi_{i}\}_{i=1}^{\ell}\subset X\text{ and }{\langle\psi_{j},\psi_{i}\rangle}_{X}=\delta_{ij}\text{ for }1\leq i,j\leq\ell.\end{aligned}\right.

Using (
2.2.7
) we have that

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{\ell}\lambda_{i}=\sum_{i=1}^{\ell}\int_{\mathscr{D}}\big|{\langle y(\mu),\psi_{i}\rangle}_{X}\big|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{\mu}}\geq\sum_{i=1}^{\ell}\int_{\mathscr{D}}\big|{\langle y({\bm{\mu}}),\chi_{i}\rangle}_{X}\big|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{\mu}}

for every
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
, where
\{\chi_{i}\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
is an arbitrary orthonormal basis in
X
.

Remark 3.7.5
.

1)

If a POD basis of rank
\ell
is determined, we set
X^{\ell}=\Span\{\psi_{1},\ldots,\psi_{\ell}\}
and introduce the following POD Galerkin scheme for (
3.7.2
) (cf. Section
3.3.1
): Find

y^{\ell}({\bm{\mu}})=\sum_{i=1}^{\ell}\mathrm{y}_{i}^{\ell}({\bm{\mu}})\psi_{i},\quad{\bm{\mu}}\in\mathscr{D},

(3.7.8)

satisfying the variational problem

a({\bm{\mu}};y^{\ell}({\bm{\mu}}),\psi)={\langle\mathcal{F}({\bm{\mu}}),\psi\rangle}_{V^{\prime},V}\quad\text{for all }\psi\in X^{\ell}.

(3.7.9)

Inserting (
3.7.8
) into (
3.7.9
) and choosing
\psi=\psi_{i}
,
1\leq i\leq\ell
in (
3.7.9
), we derive the following linear system for the coefficient vector
\mathrm{y}({\bm{\mu}})=(\mathrm{y}_{i}({\bm{\mu}}))_{1\leq i\leq\ell}\in\mathbb{R}^{\ell}
:

\displaystyle{\bm{A}}({\bm{\mu}})\mathrm{y}({\bm{\mu}})=\mathrm{\mathcal{}}F({\bm{\mu}})

with the stiffness matrix
{\bm{A}}({\bm{\mu}})=((a({\bm{\mu}};\psi_{j},\psi_{i})))\in\mathbb{R}^{\ell\times\ell}
and the right-hand side
\mathrm{f}({\bm{\mu}})=(\langle\mathcal{F}({\bm{\mu}}),\psi_{i}\rangle_{V^{\prime},V})\in\mathbb{R}^{\ell}
. Since
{\bm{A}}({\bm{\mu}})
is positive definite, existence of a unique solution
\mathrm{y}({\bm{\mu}})
follows directly for any
\ell\leq d
. This implies that also (
3.7.9
) possesses a unique solution as well.

2)

Utilizing Proposition
2.4.13
we can derive a-priori error estimates for the difference
y({\bm{\mu}})-y^{\ell}({\bm{\mu}})
; cf. Section
3.3.2
. Since we concentrate on evolution problems in this book, we does not study this error analysis here.

3)

Combining the continuous POD method for evolution problems (compare Section
3.3
) and for parametrized elliptic variational problems we can study parametrized evolution problems, where the parameter set is given as
\mathscr{D}_{T}=[0,T]\times\mathscr{D}
. To compute the POD bases the POD greedy method turns out to be very efficient; see
[
HO08
]
and Section
2.1.6
.
\blacksquare

3.7.3
The discrete POD method

In applications the weak solution to (
3.7.2
) is not known for all parameters
{\bm{\mu}}\in\mathscr{D}
, but only for a given grid in
\mathscr{D}
. For that purpose we consider a discrete variant of the POD method also lere. Let
\{{\bm{\mu}}_{j}\}_{j=1}^{n}
be a grid in
\mathscr{D}
and let
y_{j}=y({\bm{\mu}}_{j})
,
1\leq j\leq n
, denote the corresponding solutions to (
3.7.2
) for the grid points
{\bm{\mu}}_{j}
. We define the snapshot set
\mathcal{V}^{n}=\mathrm{span}\,\{y_{1},\ldots,y_{n}\}\subset V
and set
d^{n}=\dim\mathscr{V}^{n}\leq n
. Then we determine a POD basis of rank
\ell\leq d^{n}
for
\mathcal{V}^{n}
by solving

\min\sum_{j=1}^{n}\alpha_{j}\,\bigg\|y_{j}-\sum_{i=1}^{\ell}{\langle y_{j},\psi_{i}\rangle}_{X}\,\psi_{i}\bigg\|_{X}^{2}\text{ s.t. }\{\psi_{i}\}_{i=1}^{\ell}\subset X\text{ and }{\langle\psi_{j},\psi_{i}\rangle}_{X}=\delta_{ij}\text{ for }1\leq i,j\leq\ell,

(3.7.10)

where the
\alpha_{j}
’s are nonnegative weights. Let us introduce the operator
\mathcal{R}^{n}:X\to\mathscr{V}^{n}\subset X
by

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}^{n}\psi=\sum_{j=1}^{n}\alpha_{j}\,{\langle y_{j},\psi\rangle}_{X}\,y_{j}\quad\text{for }\psi\in X.

In contrast to
\mathcal{R}
introduced in (
3.7.4
) the operator
\mathcal{R}^{n}
depends on the grid
\{{\bm{\mu}}_{j}\}_{j=1}^{n}
. The image space of
\mathcal{R}^{n}
has finite dimension
d^{n}\leq n
, whereas, in general, the image space of the operator
\mathcal{R}
is infinite-dimensional. Since
\mathcal{R}^{n}
is a linear, bounded, compact, nonnegative, selfadjoint operator, there exist eigenvalues
\{\lambda_{i}^{n}\}_{i\in\mathbb{I}}
and orthonormal eigenfunctions
\{\psi_{i}^{n}\}_{i\in\mathbb{I}}
satisfying

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}^{n}\psi_{i}^{n}=\lambda_{i}^{n}\psi_{i}^{n}\text{ for }i=1,\ldots,d^{n},\quad\lambda_{1}^{n}\geq\ldots\geq\ldots\geq\lambda_{d^{n}}^{n}>0.

The solution to (
3.7.10
) is given by
\{\psi_{i}^{n}\}_{i=1}^{\ell}
and we have

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{n}\alpha_{j}\,\bigg\|y_{j}-\sum_{i=1}^{\ell}{\langle y_{j},\psi_{i}^{n}\rangle}_{X}\psi_{i}^{n}\bigg\|_{X}^{2}=\sum_{i=\ell+1}^{d^{n}}\lambda_{i}^{n}.

Remark 3.7.6
.

1)

Methods of snapshot

[
Sir87
]
: Let us introduce the
(n\times n)
diagonal matrix
{\bm{D}}=\mathrm{diag}\,(\alpha_{1},\ldots,\alpha_{n})
. We supply
\mathbb{R}^{n}
with the weighted inner product

\displaystyle{\langle\mathrm{u},\mathrm{v}\rangle}_{\bm{D}}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\alpha_{i}\mathrm{u}_{i}\mathrm{v}_{i}=\mathrm{u}^{\top}{\bm{D}}\mathrm{v}\quad\text{for }\mathrm{u}=(u_{i}),\,\mathrm{v}=(v_{i})\in\mathbb{R}^{n}.

If the
\alpha_{i}
’s are quadrature weights corresponding to the parameter grid
\{{\bm{\mu}}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
then the inner product
\langle\cdot\,,\cdot\rangle_{\bm{D}}
is a discrete version of the inner product in
L^{2}(\mathscr{D})
. We define the symmetric nonnegative matrix
\mathcal{K}^{n}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
with the elements
\langle y_{i},y_{j}\rangle_{X}
,
1\leq i,j\leq n
, and consider the eigenvalue problem

\mathcal{K}^{n}\phi_{i}^{n}=\lambda_{i}^{n}\phi_{i}^{n},~1\leq i\leq\ell\quad\text{and}\quad{\langle\phi_{i}^{n},\phi_{j}^{n}\rangle}_{\bm{D}}=\delta_{ij},~1\leq i,j\leq\ell.

(3.7.11)

From the singular value decomposition it follows that
\mathcal{K}^{n}
has the same eigenvalues
\{\lambda_{i}^{n}\}_{i\in\mathbb{I}}
as the operator
\mathcal{R}^{n}
. Furthermore, the POD basis functions are given by the formula

\psi_{i}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\lambda_{i}^{n}}}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\alpha_{j}\Phi_{ji}^{n}y_{j}\quad\text{for }i=1,\ldots,\ell\leq d^{n},

(3.7.12)

where
\Phi_{ji}^{n}
denotes the
j
-th component of the eigenvector
\phi_{i}^{n}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
.

2)

Usually, the snapshots
\{y_{j}\}_{j=1}^{n}
has to computed by a numerical approximation scheme. Here we can introduce a Galerkin discretization as in Section
3.4.1
.

3)

Following Section
3.5.4
we can introduce a POD Galerkin scheme and discuss an a-priori error analysis utilizing Proposition
2.4.17
; cf. Section
3.5.5
. However, we will not do it in this book, because our focus is on evolution problems; cf. Remark
3.7.5
-2).
\blacksquare

3.7.4
Extension to nonlinear problems

Let us give a few comments how to deal with parametrized nonlinear elliptic variational problems. Suppose that for any
{\bm{\mu}}\in\mathscr{D}
the mapping
\mathcal{N}({\bm{\mu}};\cdot):V\to V^{\prime}
is a nonlinear, locally Lipschitz-continuous mapping satisfying

{\langle\mathcal{N}({\bm{\mu}};\phi)-\mathcal{N}({\bm{\mu}};\varphi),\phi-\varphi\rangle}_{V^{\prime},V}\geq 0\quad\text{for all }\phi,\varphi\in V\text{ and for all }{\bm{\mu}}\in\mathscr{D},

(3.7.13)

i.e.,
\mathcal{N}({\bm{\mu}};\cdot)
is monotone for any
{\bm{\mu}}\in\mathscr{D}
. Instead of (
3.7.2
) we consider

a({\bm{\mu}};y({\bm{\mu}}),\varphi)+{\langle\mathcal{N}({\bm{\mu}};y({\bm{\mu}})),\varphi\rangle}_{V^{\prime},V}={\langle\mathcal{f}({\bm{\mu}}),\varphi\rangle}_{V^{\prime},V}\quad\text{for all }\varphi\in V.

(3.7.14)

Example 3.7.7
.

Let us give an example for a semilinear problem satisfying (
3.7.13
). Suppose that
\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{\mathfrak{n}}
,
\mathfrak{n}\in\{1,2,3\}
, is a bounded and open set with Lipschitz-continuous boundary
\Gamma=\partial\Omega
. We consider the
elliptic partial differential equation

-\mu_{1}\Delta y+\mu_{2}y+\mu_{3}y^{3}=f\text{ in }\Omega\quad\text{and}\quad\mu_{1}\frac{\partial y}{\partial{\bm{n}}}+\mu_{4}y=g\text{ on }\Gamma,

(3.7.15)

where
f\in L^{2}(\Omega)
,
g\in L^{2}(\Gamma)
and

\displaystyle\mathscr{D}=\big\{{\bm{\mu}}=(\mu_{1},\ldots,\mu_{4})\in\mathbb{R}^{4}\,\big|\,{\bm{\mu}}_{\mathsf{a}}\leq{\bm{\mu}}\leq{\bm{\mu}}_{\mathsf{b}}\text{ in }\mathbb{R}^{4}\big\}

with
0<\mu_{a}\leq\mu_{b}
. A
weak solution
to (
3.7.15
) satisfies
y\in V=H^{1}(\Omega)
and

\int_{\Omega}\mu_{1}\nabla y\cdot\nabla\varphi+\big(\mu_{2}y+\mu_{3}y^{3}\big)\varphi\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}}+\int_{\Gamma}\mu_{4}y\varphi\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{s}}=\int_{\Omega}f\varphi\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}}+\int_{\Gamma}g\varphi\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{s}}

(3.7.16)

for all
\varphi\in V
. Next we express (
3.7.16
) in the form (
3.7.14
). For that purpose we utilize the parametrized bilinear form
a(\mu;\cdot\,,\cdot):V\times V\to\mathbb{R}
given by

\displaystyle a({\bm{\mu}};\phi,\varphi)=\int_{\Omega}\mu_{1}\nabla\phi\cdot\nabla\varphi+\mu_{2}\phi\varphi\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}}+\int_{\Gamma}\mu_{4}\phi\varphi\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{s}}\quad\text{for }\phi,\varphi\in V\text{ and }{\bm{\mu}}\in\mathscr{D}.

Notice that this bilinear form satisfies (
3.7.1
). Moreover, let the parameter independent right-hand side be given as

\displaystyle{\langle\mathcal{F},\varphi\rangle}_{V^{\prime},V}=\int_{\Omega}f\varphi\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}}+\int_{\Gamma}g\varphi\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{s}}\quad\text{for }\varphi\in V.

Finally, we define the nonlinearity

\displaystyle{\langle\mathcal{N}({\bm{\mu}};\varphi),\phi\rangle}_{V^{\prime},V}=\mu_{3}\int_{\Omega}\varphi^{3}\phi\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}}\quad\text{for }\varphi,\phi\in V\text{ and }{\bm{\mu}}\in\mathscr{D}.

Then a weak solution to (
3.7.15
) satisfies the variational formulation (
3.7.14
). Recall that
\varphi\in V
implies
\varphi\in L^{6}(\Omega)
. Consequently,
\varphi^{3}\in H=L^{2}(\Omega)\subset V^{\prime}
. Let
\phi,\varphi\in V
and
\chi=\phi-\varphi\in V
. From
\mu_{3}\geq\mu_{a}>0
we infer that

\displaystyle{\langle\mathcal{N}({\bm{\mu}};\phi)-\mathcal{N}({\bm{\mu}};\varphi),\chi\rangle}_{V^{\prime},V}

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega}\mu_{3}\big(\phi^{3}-\varphi^{3}\big)\chi\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}}=\mu_{3}\int_{\Omega}\bigg(\int_{0}^{1}3(\varphi+\tau\chi)^{2}\chi\mathrm{d}\tau\big)\chi\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}}

\displaystyle=\mu_{3}\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\Omega}(\varphi+\tau\chi)^{2}\chi^{2}d\tau\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}}\geq 0

holds true. Thus, (
3.7.13
) is satisfied.
\blacklozenge

Remark 3.7.8
.

If a solution
y({\bm{\mu}})
to (
3.7.14
) is given, then a POD basis can be computed as described above for the linear elliptic variational problem (
3.7.2
).
\blacksquare

3.8
Proofs of Section
3

3.8.1
Proofs of Section
3.2

Proof of Theorem
3.2.4
.

For a proof of the existence of a unique weak solution we refer to
[
DL00
, pp. 512-520]
. The regularity result follows from
[
DL00
, pp. 532-533]
and
[
Eva08
, pp. 360-364]
. To prove the a-priori error estimate (
3.2.5
) we choose
\varphi=y(t)
in (
3.2.4a
). Utilizing the coercivity estimate (
3.2.1b
) and Young’s inequality we derive

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\,\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\,{\|y(t)\|}_{H}^{2}+\gamma_{1}\,{\|y(t)\|}_{V}^{2}-\gamma_{2}\|y(t)\|_{H}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\big({\|\mathcal{F}(t)\|}_{V^{\prime}}+{\|(\mathcal{B}u)(t)\|}_{V^{\prime}}\big){\|y(t)\|}_{V}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2\gamma_{1}}\big({\|\mathcal{F}(t)\|}_{V^{\prime}}^{2}+{\|(\mathcal{B}u)(t)\|}_{V^{\prime}}^{2}\big)+\frac{\gamma_{1}}{2}\,{\|y(t)\|}_{V}^{2},

which implies

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\,{\|y(t)\|}_{H}^{2}+\gamma_{1}\,{\|y(t)\|}_{V}^{2}\leq 2\gamma_{2}\|y(t)\|^{2}_{H}+\frac{2}{\gamma_{1}}\big({\|\mathcal{F}(t)\|}_{V^{\prime}}^{2}+{\|(\mathcal{B}u)(t)\|}_{V^{\prime}}^{2}\big).

(3.8.1)

We insert the embedding estimate (
2.4.1
) and infer that

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\,{\|y(t)\|}_{H}^{2}\leq c_{1}\,{\|y(t)\|}_{H}^{2}+\frac{2}{\gamma_{1}}\big({\|\mathcal{F}(t)\|}_{V^{\prime}}^{2}+{\|(\mathcal{B}u)(t)\|}_{V^{\prime}}^{2}\big)

with
c_{1}=\max(2\gamma_{2}-\gamma_{1}/c_{V}^{2},0)\geq 0
. By applying the Gronwall Lemma (Proposition
A.1.32
), we find

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} {\|y(t)\|}_{H}^{2}&\leq e^{c_{1}t}\bigg({\|y_{\circ}\|}_{H}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\frac{2}{\gamma_{1}}\big({\|\mathcal{F}(s)\|}_{V^{\prime}}^{2}+{\|(\mathcal{B}u)(s)\|}_{V^{\prime}}^{2}\big)\,\mathrm{d}s\bigg)\\
&\leq e^{c_{1}t}\bigg({\|y_{\circ}\|}_{H}^{2}+\frac{2}{\gamma_{1}}\left({\|\mathcal{F}\|}_{L^{2}(0,T;V^{\prime})}^{2}+{\|\mathcal{B}\|}_{\mathscr{L}(U;L^{2}(0,T;V^{\prime}))}^{2}{\|u\|}_{\mathscr{U}}^{2}\right)\bigg)\end{aligned}

(3.8.2)

which implies

{\|y(t)\|}_{H}^{2}\leq e^{c_{1}t}\Big({\|y_{\circ}\|}_{H}^{2}+c_{2}\big({\|\mathcal{F}\|}_{L^{2}(0,T;V^{\prime})}^{2}+{\|u\|}_{\mathscr{U}}^{2}\big)\Big)

(3.8.3)

with
c_{2}=2\max(1,\|\mathcal{B}\|_{\mathscr{L}(U;L^{2}(0,T;V^{\prime}))}^{2})/\gamma_{1}>0
. Integrating (
3.8.1
) over
[0,T]
and dropping the nonnegative term
\|y(T)\|_{H}^{2}
, it follows that

\displaystyle{\|y\|}_{L^{2}(0,T;V)}^{2}

\displaystyle~\leq\frac{1}{\gamma_{1}}\bigg({\|y_{\circ}\|}_{H}^{2}+c_{2}\,\big({\|\mathcal{F}\|}_{L^{2}(0,T;V^{\prime})}^{2}+{\|u\|}_{\mathscr{U}}^{2}\big)+2\gamma_{2}\int_{0}^{T}\|y(t)\|_{H}^{2}~\mathrm{d}t\bigg)

We insert the estimate (
3.8.2
), which results in

\displaystyle{\|y\|}_{L^{2}(0,T;V)}^{2}\leq c_{3}\big({\|y_{\circ}\|}_{H}^{2}+{\|\mathcal{F}\|}_{L^{2}(0,T;V^{\prime})}^{2}+{\|u\|}_{\mathscr{U}}^{2}\big),

(3.8.4)

where the constant is given by

\displaystyle c_{3}=\frac{(1+2\gamma_{2}Te^{c_{1}T})\max(1,c_{2})}{\gamma_{1}}.

Let
\varphi\in V
with
\|\varphi\|_{V}=1
be arbitrary. Using (
3.2.4a
) and (
3.2.1a
), we estimate

\displaystyle{\langle y_{t}(t),\varphi\rangle}_{V^{\prime},V}

\displaystyle={\langle(\mathcal{F}+\mathcal{B}u)(t),\varphi\rangle}_{V^{\prime},V}-a(t;y(t),\varphi)

\displaystyle\leq{\|(\mathcal{F}+\mathcal{B}u)(t)\|}_{V^{\prime}}{\|\varphi\|}_{V}+\gamma\,{\|y(t)\|}_{V}{\|\varphi\|}_{V}

\displaystyle\leq{\|\mathcal{F}(t)\|}_{V^{\prime}}+{\|(\mathcal{B}u)(t)\|}_{V^{\prime}}+\gamma\,{\|y(t)\|}_{V}

for almost all
t\in(0,T)
. Thus, it holds

\displaystyle{\|y_{t}(t)\|}_{V^{\prime}}\leq{\|\mathcal{F}(t)\|}_{V^{\prime}}+{\|(\mathcal{B}u)(t)\|}_{V^{\prime}}+\gamma\,{\|y(t)\|}_{V}

for almost all
t\in(0,T)
. Consequently, using (
3.8.4
) yields

\displaystyle{\|y_{t}\|}_{L^{2}(0,T;V^{\prime})}^{2}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{T}{\|y_{t}(t)\|}_{V^{\prime}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t\leq\int_{0}^{T}3{\|\mathcal{F}(t)\|}_{V^{\prime}}^{2}+3\,{\|(\mathcal{B}u)(t)\|}_{V^{\prime}}^{2}+3\gamma\,{\|y(t)\|}_{V}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t

(3.8.5)

\displaystyle=3\,{\|\mathcal{F}\|}_{L^{2}(0,T;V^{\prime})}^{2}+3\,{\|\mathcal{B}u\|}_{L^{2}(0,T;V^{\prime})}^{2}+3\gamma\,{\|y\|}_{L^{2}(0,T;V)}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 3\,{\|\mathcal{F}\|}_{L^{2}(0,T;V^{\prime})}^{2}+3\,{\|\mathcal{B}\|}_{\mathscr{L}({\mathscr{U}};L^{2}(0,T;V^{\prime}))}^{2}{\|u\|}_{\mathscr{U}}^{2}+3\gamma\,{\|y\|}_{L^{2}(0,T;V)}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq c_{4}\big({\|y_{\circ}\|}_{H}^{2}+{\|\mathcal{F}\|}_{L^{2}(0,T;V^{\prime})}^{2}+{\|u\|}_{\mathscr{U}}^{2}\big)

with
c_{4}=3\gamma c_{3}+3\max(1,\|\mathcal{B}\|_{\mathscr{L}(U;L^{2}(0,T;V^{\prime}))}^{2})
. From (
3.8.4
) and (
3.8.5
) we get

\displaystyle{\|y\|}_{\mathscr{Y}}

\displaystyle=\big({\|y_{t}\|}_{L^{2}(0,T;V^{\prime})}^{2}+{\|y\|}_{L^{2}(0,T;V)}^{2}\big)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\left(c_{4}+c_{3}\right)^{1/2}\big({\|y_{\circ}\|}_{H}^{2}+{\|\mathcal{F}\|}_{L^{2}(0,T;V^{\prime})}^{2}+{\|u\|}_{\mathscr{U}}^{2}\big)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\left(c_{4}+c_{3}\right)^{1/2}\big({\|y_{\circ}\|}_{H}+{\|\mathcal{F}\|}_{L^{2}(0,T;V^{\prime})}+{\|u\|}_{\mathscr{U}}\big).

Setting
C=\left(c_{4}+c_{3}\right)^{1/2}
we obtain the a-priori estimate (
3.2.5
).
\Box

3.8.2
Proofs of Section
3.3

Proof of Lemma
3.3.3
.
Clearly,
{\bm{M}}^{\ell}
is symmetric. Let
\mathrm{v}=(\mathrm{v}_{i})^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{\ell}
be chosen arbitrarily. We set

\displaystyle v^{\ell}=\sum_{i=1}^{\ell}\mathrm{v}_{i}\psi_{i}\in X^{\ell}\subset V.

Then we derive

\displaystyle\mathrm{v}^{\top}{\bm{M}}^{\ell}\mathrm{v}=\sum_{i=1}^{\ell}\sum_{j=1}^{\ell}\mathrm{v}_{i}\mathrm{v}_{j}\,{\langle\psi_{j},\psi_{i}\rangle}_{H}={\|v^{\ell}\|}_{H}^{2}\geq 0.

Further,
\mathrm{v}^{\top}{\bm{M}}^{\ell}\mathrm{v}=0
holds if and only if
\mathrm{v}=0
is true because
\{\psi_{1},\ldots,\psi_{\ell}\}
are linearily independent. Thus, the matrix
{\bm{M}}^{\ell}
is positive definite. From (
3.2.1b
) and the assumption that
\gamma_{2}=0
, we derive

\displaystyle\mathrm{v}^{\top}{\bm{A}}^{\ell}(t)\mathrm{v}=\sum_{i=1}^{\ell}\sum_{j=1}^{\ell}\mathrm{v}_{i}\mathrm{v}_{j}a(\psi_{j},\psi_{i})\geq\gamma_{1}\,{\|v^{\ell}\|}_{V}^{2}\geq 0

which implies (
3.3.6
). From (
3.3.6
) we infer that
{\bm{A}}^{\ell}(t)\mathrm{v}\neq 0
holds for any
\mathrm{v}\in\mathbb{R}^{\ell}\setminus\{0\}
in
[0,T]
. Thus, the columns of
{\bm{A}}^{\ell}(t)
are linearly independent, so that
{\bm{A}}^{\ell}(t)
is a regular matrix in
[0,T]
.
\Box

Proof of Theorem
3.3.5
.

Since
V^{\ell}
is a finite-dimensional subspace of
V
, the proof of the existence of a unique solution
y\in H^{1}(0,T;V^{\ell})\subset H^{1}(0,T;V)
to (
3.3.2
) follows again from
[
DL00
, pp. 512-520]
. To prove the a-priori estimate we proceed as in the proof of Theorem
3.2.4
and derive

{\|y^{\ell}\|}_{L^{2}(0,T;V)}\leq c_{1}\big({\|\mathcal{P}^{\ell}y_{\circ}\|}_{H}+{\|\mathcal{F}\|}_{L^{2}(0,T;V^{\prime})}+{\|u\|}_{\mathscr{U}}\big);

(3.8.6)

where
c_{1}=\max(1/\sqrt{\gamma}_{1},2/\gamma_{1},2\|\mathcal{B}\|_{\mathscr{L}({\mathscr{U}};L^{2}(0,T;V^{\prime}))}/\gamma_{1})>0
. From (
3.2.1a
), (
3.3.2a
) and
\mathcal{F},\mathcal{B}u\in L^{2}(0,T;V^{\prime})
we infer that

y_{t}^{\ell}(t)={\langle(\mathcal{F}+\mathcal{B}u)(t),\cdot\rangle}_{V^{\prime},V}-a(y^{\ell}(t),\cdot)\text{ in }(V^{\ell})^{\prime}

(3.8.7)

holds for almost all
t\in[0,T]
. Hence, (
3.8.7
) and (
3.2.1a
) imply that

\displaystyle{\langle y_{t}^{\ell}(t),\varphi\rangle_{V^{\prime},V}}

\displaystyle={\langle(\mathcal{F}+\mathcal{B}u)(t),\varphi\rangle}_{V^{\prime},V}-a(y^{\ell}(t),\varphi)

\displaystyle\leq{\|\mathcal{F}(t)\|}_{V^{\prime}}+{\|(\mathcal{B}u)(t)\|}_{V^{\prime}}+\gamma\,{\|y^{\ell}(t)\|}_{V}

holds for all
\varphi\in V^{\ell}
with
{\|\varphi\|}_{V}=1
and almost all
t\in[0,T]
. Using (
3.3.3
) this implies

\displaystyle{\|y_{t}^{\ell}(t)\|}_{V^{\prime}}=\|y_{t}^{\ell}(t)\|_{(V^{\ell})^{\prime}}\leq{\|\mathcal{F}(t)\|}_{V^{\prime}}+{\|(\mathcal{B}u)(t)\|}_{V^{\prime}}+\gamma\,{\|y^{\ell}(t)\|}_{V}.

(3.8.8)

Hence, using (
3.8.6
) and (
3.8.8
) yields

\displaystyle{\|y_{t}^{\ell}\|}_{L^{2}(0,T;V^{\prime})}^{2}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{T}{\|y_{t}^{\ell}(t)\|}_{V^{\prime}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t

(3.8.9)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{T}3\,{\|\mathcal{F}(t)\|}_{V^{\prime}}^{2}+3\,{\|(\mathcal{B}u)(t)\|}_{V^{\prime}}^{2}+3\gamma\,{\|y^{\ell}(t)\|}_{V}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq 3\,{\|\mathcal{F}\|}_{L^{2}(0,T;V^{\prime})}^{2}+3\,{\|\mathcal{B}\|}_{\mathscr{L}({\mathscr{U}},L^{2}(0,T;V^{\prime}))}^{2}{\|u\|}_{\mathscr{U}}^{2}+3\gamma\,{\|y^{\ell}\|}_{L^{2}(0,T;V)}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq c_{2}\big({\|\mathcal{P}^{\ell}y_{\circ}\|}_{H}^{2}+{\|\mathcal{F}\|}_{L^{2}(0,T;V^{\prime})}^{2}+{\|u\|}_{\mathscr{U}}^{2}\big),

where
c_{2}=9\gamma\,c_{1}+3\max(1,\|\mathcal{B}\|_{\mathscr{L}({\mathscr{U}},L^{2}(0,T;V^{\prime}))}^{2})
. Now, we directly get

\displaystyle{\|y^{\ell}\|}_{W(0,T)}\leq C\left({\|\mathcal{P}^{\ell}y_{\circ}\|}_{H}+{\|\mathcal{F}\|}_{L^{2}(0,T;V^{\prime})}+{\|u\|}_{\mathscr{U}}\right)

follows directly with
C=(3c_{1}^{2}+c_{2})^{1/2}
. This implies (
3.3.8
) due to the isomorphism between
H^{1}(0,T;V)
and
{\mathscr{Y}}
, cf. Lemma
3.3.7
.
\Box

Proof of Lemma
3.3.7
.
Let
y\in W(0,T)
be given. Since
V
and
H
have the same finite dimension
\mathfrak{n}
,
V^{\prime}
has this dimension as well and the linear and injective embeddings
V\hookrightarrow H\hookrightarrow V^{\prime}
are thus surjective as well. For almost every
t\in(0,T)
, it holds
y_{t}(t)\in V^{\prime}
and, due to the surjectivity, there exists
\tilde{y}(t)\in V
whose Gelfand embedding into
V^{\prime}
is given by
\langle\tilde{y}(t),\cdot\rangle_{H}
and is identical to
y_{t}(t)
. Because of
y\in H^{1}(0,T;V^{\prime})\hookrightarrow W(0,T)
, it holds by definition of this Sobolev space:

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{T}\langle y(t),\cdot\rangle_{H}\xi^{\prime}(t)~\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{T}y_{t}(t)\xi(t)~\mathrm{d}t=\int_{0}^{T}\langle\tilde{y}(t),\cdot\rangle_{H}\xi(t)~\mathrm{d}t\quad\text{in }V^{\prime},~\text{for all }\xi\in C_{0}^{\infty}(0,T).

Alternatively, we can write this as

\displaystyle\bigg\langle-\int_{0}^{T}y(t)\xi^{\prime}(t)\,\mathrm{d}t,\varphi\bigg\rangle_{H}=\bigg\langle\int_{0}^{T}\tilde{y}(t)\xi(t)\,\mathrm{d}t,\varphi\bigg\rangle_{H}\quad\text{for all }\xi\in C_{0}^{\infty}(0,T),\,\varphi\in V.

Since
V\hookrightarrow H
is dense (even surjective) and injective, this yields

-\int_{0}^{T}y(t)\xi^{\prime}(t)~\mathrm{d}t=\int_{0}^{T}\tilde{y}(t)\xi(t)\,\mathrm{d}t\quad\text{in }V\text{ for all }\xi\in C_{0}^{\infty}(0,T).

(3.8.10)

This proves that the
V
-derivative of
y
is given by
\tilde{y}
. After we have shown that
\tilde{y}\in L^{2}(0,T;V)
, this means
y\in H^{1}(0,T;V)
with
y_{t}=\tilde{y}
. First of all, the inverse of the surjective embedding
V\hookrightarrow H
is also continuous as a linear mapping between finite-dimensional spaces. The same holds for the inverse of
H\hookrightarrow V^{\prime}
. In other words, there is
C_{\mathfrak{n}}>0
such that the
inverse inequality

{\|\varphi\|}_{V}\leq C_{\mathfrak{n}}\,{\|\varphi\|}_{H}\quad\text{for all }\varphi\in H

(3.8.11)

and
\|\varphi\|_{H}\leq C_{\mathfrak{n}}\,\|\langle\cdot,\varphi\rangle_{H}\|_{V^{\prime}}
for all
\varphi\in H
. Notice that (
3.8.11
) does not hold if
V
and
H
have infinite dimensions, which implies that the constant
C_{\mathfrak{n}}
depends on the dimension
\mathfrak{n}
of
H
and
V
. In particular,
\lim_{\mathfrak{n}\to\infty}C_{\mathfrak{n}}=\infty
. Therefore,

{\|\tilde{y}(t)\|}_{V}\leq C_{\mathfrak{n}}\,{\|\tilde{y}(t)\|}_{H}\leq C_{\mathfrak{n}}^{2}\,{\|{\langle\cdot\,,\tilde{y}(t)\rangle}_{H}\|}_{V^{\prime}}=C_{\mathfrak{n}}^{2}\,{\|y_{t}(t)\|}_{V^{\prime}}.

(3.8.12)

From
y_{t}\in L^{2}(0,T;V^{\prime})
, it follows
\tilde{y}\in L^{2}(0,T;V)
. We have therefore defined a mapping

\displaystyle W(0,T)\to H^{1}(0,T;V),\quad y\mapsto y

which is obviously linear and, because of (
3.8.12
), continuous. Because of the nature of the mapping and the canonical injection
H^{1}(0,T;V)\hookrightarrow W(0,T)
, it is also bijective, making it an isomorphism between Hilbert spaces.
\Box

Proof of Lemma
3.3.11
.
From (
3.2.4
), (
3.3.2
), (
3.2.1a
) and
\varrho^{\ell}=\mathcal{P}^{\ell}y-y
we get for any
\psi\in X^{\ell}

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\langle\vartheta_{t}^{\ell}(t),\psi\rangle}_{V^{\prime},V}+a(t;\vartheta^{\ell}(t),\psi)={\langle\vartheta_{t}^{\ell}(t),\psi\rangle}_{V^{\prime},V}+a(t;\vartheta^{\ell}(t),\psi)

(3.8.13)

\displaystyle={\langle\mathcal{P}^{\ell}y_{t}(t),\psi\rangle}_{V^{\prime},V}+a(t;\mathcal{P}^{\ell}y(t),\psi)-{\langle y_{t}^{\ell}(t),\psi\rangle}_{V^{\prime},V}-a(t;y^{\ell}(t),\psi)

\displaystyle={\langle(\mathcal{P}^{\ell}y_{t}-y_{t})(t),\psi\rangle}_{V^{\prime},V}+a(t;\mathcal{P}^{\ell}y(t)-y(t),\psi)

\displaystyle\leq\big({\|\varrho^{\ell}_{t}(t)\|}_{V^{\prime}}+\gamma\,{\|(\varrho^{\ell}(t)\|}_{V}\big){\|\psi\|}_{V}

Choosing
\psi=\vartheta^{\ell}(t)\in X^{\ell}
for almost all
t\in[0,T]
applying Lemma
A.4.59
-4) and (
3.2.1b
) we find

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\,\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\,{\|\vartheta^{\ell}(t)\|}_{H}^{2}+\gamma_{1}\,{\|\vartheta^{\ell}(t)\|}_{V}^{2}-\gamma_{2}\|\vartheta^{\ell}(t)\|_{H}^{2}\leq\big({\|\varrho^{\ell}_{t}(t)\|}_{V^{\prime}}+\gamma\,{\|\varrho^{\ell}(t)\|}_{V}\big){\|\vartheta^{\ell}(t)\|}_{V}

in
[0,T]
almost everywhere. Applying two times Young’s inequality (cf. Lemma
A.1.30
) with
\varepsilon=\gamma_{1}/4
and multiplying by two we get

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\,{\|\vartheta^{\ell}(t)\|}_{H}^{2}+\gamma_{1}\,{\|\vartheta^{\ell}(t)\|}_{V}^{2}\leq\,2\gamma_{2}\,{\|\vartheta^{\ell}(t)\|}_{H}^{2}+\frac{2}{\gamma_{1}}\big({\|\varrho^{\ell}_{t}(t)\|}_{V^{\prime}}^{2}+\gamma^{2}\,{\|\varrho^{\ell}(t)\|}_{V}^{2}\big)

(3.8.14)

In a next step, we use the embedding estimate (
2.4.1
) for the second summand on the left-hand side and set

\displaystyle c_{1}=\max\Big(2\gamma_{2}-\frac{\gamma_{1}}{c_{V}^{2}},~0\Big),

which gives us

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\,{\|\vartheta^{\ell}(t)\|}_{H}^{2}\leq c_{1}\,{\|\vartheta^{\ell}(t)\|}_{H}^{2}+c_{2}\big({\|\varrho^{\ell}_{t}(t)\|}_{V^{\prime}}^{2}+{\|\varrho^{\ell}(t)\|}_{V}^{2}\big)

for
c_{2}=2\max\{1,\gamma^{2}\}/\gamma_{1}>0
. Now we apply the Gronwall lemma (Proposition
A.1.32
) and use
\vartheta^{\ell}(0)=0
to get

\big\|\vartheta^{\ell}(t)\big\|^{2}_{H}\leq c_{3}\int_{0}^{t}{\|\varrho^{\ell}_{t}(s)\|}_{V^{\prime}}^{2}+{\|\varrho^{\ell}(s)\|}_{V}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq c_{3}\,{\|\varrho^{\ell}\|}_{\mathscr{Y}}^{2}\quad\text{a.e. in }[0,T]

(3.8.15)

with
c_{3}=c_{2}\exp(c_{1}T)>0
. From (
3.8.15
) it follows that

{\|\vartheta^{\ell}\|}_{C([0,T];H)}^{2}\leq c_{3}\,{\|\varrho^{\ell}\|}_{\mathscr{Y}}^{2}.

(3.8.16)

We continue by integrating (
3.8.14
) over
[0,T]
dropping the non-negative term
\|\vartheta^{\ell}(T)\|_{H}^{2}
and using again
\vartheta^{\ell}(0)=0
in
H
. This gives us

\displaystyle\gamma_{1}\,{\|\vartheta^{\ell}\|}_{L^{2}(0,T;V)}^{2}\leq 2\gamma_{2}\int_{0}^{T}\|\vartheta^{\ell}(t)\|_{H}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t+c_{2}\,{\|\varrho^{\ell}\|}_{\mathscr{Y}}^{2}.

Inserting the estimate (
3.8.16
) for
\|\vartheta^{\ell}(t)\|_{H}^{2}
we derive

{\|\vartheta^{\ell}\|}_{L^{2}(0,T;V)}^{2}\leq c_{4}\,{\|\varrho^{\ell}\|}_{\mathscr{Y}}^{2}

(3.8.17)

for the constant
c_{4}=(2\gamma_{2}c_{3}T+c_{2})/\gamma_{1}>0
. Using (
3.2.1a
) and (
3.3.12
) we find that

\displaystyle{\langle\vartheta_{t}^{\ell}(t),\psi\rangle}_{V^{\prime},V}

\displaystyle={\langle\mathcal{P}^{\ell}y_{t}(t),\psi\rangle}_{V^{\prime},V}-{\langle y^{\ell}_{t}(t),\psi\rangle}_{V^{\prime},V}

\displaystyle={\langle\mathcal{P}^{\ell}y_{t}(t)-y_{t}(t),\psi\rangle}_{V^{\prime},V}-a(t;y(t)-y^{\ell}(t),\psi)

\displaystyle\leq{\|\mathcal{P}^{\ell}y_{t}(t)-y_{t}(t)\|}_{V^{\prime}}{\|\psi\|}_{V}+\gamma\,{\|\varrho^{\ell}(t)+\vartheta^{\ell}(t)\|}_{V}{\|\psi\|}_{V}

\displaystyle\leq c_{5}\left({\|\varrho_{t}^{\ell}(t)\|}_{V^{\prime}}+{\|\varrho^{\ell}(t)\|}_{V}+{\|\vartheta^{\ell}(t)\|}_{V}\right)

with
c_{5}=\max\{1,\gamma\}>0
. Hence, applying (
3.3.3
) yields

{\|\vartheta_{t}^{\ell}(t)\|}_{V^{\prime}}={\|\vartheta_{t}^{\ell}(t)\|}_{(V^{\ell})^{\prime}}\leq c_{5}\left({\|\varrho_{t}^{\ell}(t)\|}_{V^{\prime}}+{\|\varrho^{\ell}(t)\|}_{V}+{\|\vartheta^{\ell}(t)\|}_{V}\right).

Thus, (
3.8.17
) implies

\displaystyle{\|\vartheta_{t}^{\ell}\|}_{L^{2}(0,T;V^{\prime})}^{2}=\int_{0}^{T}{\|\vartheta_{t}^{\ell}(t)\|}_{V^{\prime}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t\leq 3c_{5}^{2}\big({\|\varrho_{t}^{\ell}\|}_{\mathscr{Y}}^{2}+{\|\vartheta^{\ell}\|}_{L^{2}(0,T,V)}^{2}\big)\leq c_{6}\,{\|\varrho^{\ell}\|}_{\mathscr{Y}}

for
c_{6}=3c_{5}^{2}\max\{1,c_{4}\}>0
. Together with (
3.8.17
) this implies

{\|\vartheta^{\ell}\|}_{{\mathscr{Y}}}^{2}={\|\vartheta^{\ell}\|}_{L^{2}(0,T;V)}^{2}+{\|\vartheta^{\ell}_{t}\|}_{L^{2}(0,T;V^{\prime})}^{2}\leq(c_{4}+c_{6})\,{\|\varrho^{\ell}\|}_{\mathscr{Y}}.

(3.8.18)

Thus, (
3.3.14
) follows from (
3.8.18
) with
C=c_{4}+c_{6}
.
\Box

Proof of Lemma
3.3.13
.
We know that
\mathcal{P}^{\ell}
is an
H
-orthonormal projection in
X=H
or
X=V
. For arbitary
\psi,\phi\in H
, we set
\psi^{H}=\mathcal{P}^{\ell}\psi
,
\phi^{H}=\mathcal{P}^{\ell}\phi\in X^{\ell}
and
\psi^{\bot}=\psi-\psi^{H}
,
\phi^{\bot}=\phi-\phi^{H}
. It follows that
\langle\psi^{H},\phi^{\bot}\rangle_{H}=0
and
\langle\psi^{\bot},\phi^{H}\rangle_{H}=0
holds true. Hence,

\displaystyle{\langle\mathcal{P}^{\ell}\psi,\phi\rangle}_{H}={\langle\psi^{H},\phi^{H}+\phi^{\bot}\rangle}_{H}

\displaystyle={\langle\psi^{H},\phi^{H}\rangle}_{H}+{\langle\psi^{H},\phi^{\bot}\rangle}_{H}

(3.8.19)

\displaystyle={\langle\psi^{H},\phi^{H}\rangle}_{H}+{\langle\psi^{\bot},\phi^{H}\rangle}_{H}={\langle\psi,\mathcal{P}^{\ell}\phi\rangle}_{H}.

Following
[
Sin14
]
and using
y\in H^{1}(0,T;H)
we observe that

\displaystyle{\langle\mathcal{P}^{\ell}y_{t}(t),\varphi\rangle}_{H}=\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\,{\langle\mathcal{P}^{\ell}y(t),\varphi\rangle}_{H}=\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}{\langle y(t),\mathcal{P}^{\ell}\varphi\rangle}_{H}={\langle y_{t}(t),\mathcal{P}^{\ell}\varphi\rangle}_{H}

for all
\varphi\in H
and for almost all
t\in[0,T]
. In particular, we find that

{\langle\mathcal{P}^{\ell}y_{t}(t),\psi\rangle}_{H}={\langle y_{t}(t),\psi\rangle}_{H}\quad\text{for all }\psi\in X^{\ell}\text{ and in }[0,T]\text{ a.e.}

(3.8.20)

Thus, we can stray from the proof of Lemma
3.3.11
by making a different estimate than (
3.8.13
) in this case:

\displaystyle{\langle\vartheta_{t}^{\ell}(t),\psi\rangle}_{H}+a(t;\vartheta^{\ell}(t),\psi)

\displaystyle={\langle y_{t}(t),\psi\rangle}_{H}+a(t;\mathcal{P}^{\ell}y(t),\psi)-{\langle y_{t}^{\ell}(t),\psi\rangle}_{H}-a(t;y^{\ell}(t),\psi)

(3.8.21)

\displaystyle=a(t;(\mathcal{P}^{\ell}y-y)(t),\psi)\leq\gamma\,{\|(\mathcal{P}^{\ell}y-y)(t)\|}_{V}{\|\psi\|}_{V}

for all
\psi\in X^{\ell}
and in
[0,T]
almost everywhere. Note that no time derivatives of
\mathcal{P}^{\ell}y-y
occur in this case, in contrast to (
3.8.13
). The same computations as in the proof of Lemma
3.3.11
and Remark
3.3.12
-2) lead to (
3.3.20
).
\Box

Proof of Theorem
3.3.18
.
Define the set
\mathscr{S}=\{u\in{\mathscr{U}}\,|\,\|u\|_{\mathscr{U}}=1\}
. Note that

\displaystyle{\|\mathcal{S}^{\ell}-\mathcal{S}\|}_{\mathscr{L}({\mathscr{U}},{\mathscr{Y}})}=\sup_{u\in\mathscr{S}}{\|\mathcal{S}^{\ell}u-\mathcal{S}u\|}_{\mathscr{Y}}.

Consequently,
\mathcal{S}^{\ell}
converges to
\mathcal{S}
in
\mathscr{L}({\mathscr{U}},{\mathscr{Y}})
if and only if
\mathcal{S}^{\ell}u
converges to
\mathcal{S}u
uniformly on
\mathscr{S}
.

Suppose that
\{\mathcal{S}^{\ell}u\}_{\ell\in\mathbb{N}}
does not converge uniformly for all
u\in\mathscr{S}
. Then there is an
\varepsilon>0
such that for any
\ell_{\circ}=\ell_{\circ}(\varepsilon)\in\mathbb{N}
there is an
\ell\geq\ell_{\circ}
and a
u^{\ell}\in\mathscr{S}
satisfying

{\|\mathcal{S}^{\ell}u^{\ell}-\mathcal{S}u^{\ell}\|}_{\mathscr{Y}}\geq 3\varepsilon,

i.e., we can choose a sequence
\{u^{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\subset\mathscr{S}
and a subsequence
\{\mathcal{S}^{\ell_{n}}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
of
\{\mathcal{S}^{\ell}\}_{\ell\in\mathbb{N}}
such that

{\|\mathcal{S}^{\ell_{n}}u^{n}-\mathcal{S}u^{n}\|}_{\mathscr{Y}}\geq 3\varepsilon

(3.8.22)

holds for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
. Since
{\mathscr{U}}
is finite-dimensional the set
\mathscr{S}
is compact. Thus, without loss of generality we can assume that there is a limit point
u_{\circ}\in\mathscr{S}
with
u^{n}\to u_{\circ}
in
{\mathscr{U}}
as
n\to\infty
.

The continuity of the operator
\mathcal{S}
immediately implies that there is a
n_{1}\in\mathbb{N}
such that

{\|\mathcal{S}u^{n}-\mathcal{S}u_{\circ}\|}_{{\mathscr{Y}}}<\varepsilon\quad\text{for all }n\geq n_{1}.

(3.8.23)

Since
\mathcal{S}^{\ell}
is linear and bounded independently of
\ell
, there exists a constant
C>0
such that

\displaystyle{\|\mathcal{S}^{\ell}u_{1}-\mathcal{S}^{\ell}u_{2}\|}_{\mathscr{Y}}\leq C\,{\|u_{1}-u_{2}\|}_{\mathscr{U}}

for all
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
and for all
u_{1},u_{2}\in{\mathscr{U}}
. Setting
\delta=\varepsilon/C>0
it holds

{\|\mathcal{S}^{\ell}u_{1}-\mathcal{S}^{\ell}u_{2}\|}_{\mathscr{Y}}<\varepsilon

for all
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
and for all
u_{1},u_{2}\in{\mathscr{U}}
with
{\|u_{1}-u_{2}\|}_{\mathscr{U}}<\delta
.
Using again that
u^{n}\to u_{\circ}
in
{\mathscr{U}}
as
n\to\infty
we get the existence of a
n_{2}\in\mathbb{N}
such that

\displaystyle{\|u^{n}-u_{\circ}\|}_{\mathscr{U}}\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{C}\quad\text{for all }n\geq n_{2}.

In total, we thus get

{\|\mathcal{S}^{\ell_{n}}u^{n}-\mathcal{S}^{\ell_{n}}u_{\circ}\|}_{\mathscr{Y}}<\varepsilon\quad\text{for all }n\geq n_{2}.

(3.8.24)

Moreover, applying Theoremy
3.3.16
yields the existence of a
n_{3}\in\mathbb{N}
such that

{\|\mathcal{S}^{\ell_{n}}u_{\circ}-\mathcal{S}u_{\circ}\|}_{{\mathscr{Y}}}<\varepsilon\quad\text{for all }n\geq n_{3}.

(3.8.25)

Therefore, we derive from (
3.8.23
), (
3.8.24
) and (
3.8.25
) that

\displaystyle{\|\mathcal{S}^{\ell_{n}}u^{n}-\mathcal{S}u^{n}\|}_{\mathscr{Y}}

\displaystyle\leq{\|\mathcal{S}^{\ell_{n}}u^{n}-\mathcal{S}^{\ell_{n}}u_{\circ}\|}_{\mathscr{Y}}+{\|\mathcal{S}^{\ell_{n}}u_{\circ}-\mathcal{S}u_{\circ}\|}_{\mathscr{Y}}+{\|\mathcal{S}u_{\circ}-\mathcal{S}u^{n}\|}_{\mathscr{Y}}

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon+\varepsilon+\varepsilon=3\varepsilon

which contradicts (
3.8.22
)
\Box

3.8.3
Proofs of Section
3.4

Proof of Theorem
3.4.13
.
We set
e^{h\ell}(t;u)=y^{h}(t;u)-y^{h\ell}(t;u)\in V^{h}
for the error between
y^{h}
and
y^{h\ell}
. Then we derive from (
3.4.4
) and (
3.4.23
) that for
t\in[0,T]
and
\varphi\in V^{h}

\displaystyle{\langle e^{h\ell}_{t}(t;u),\varphi\rangle}_{V^{\prime},V}+a(t;e^{h\ell}(t;u),\varphi)=-{\langle r^{h\ell}(t;u),\varphi\rangle}_{V^{\prime},V}

with the residual

\displaystyle r^{h\ell}(t;u)={\langle y^{h\ell}_{t}(t;u)-(\mathcal{F}+\mathcal{B}u)(t),\cdot\rangle}_{V^{\prime},V}+a(t;y^{h\ell}(t;u),\cdot)\in(V^{h})^{\prime}.

Choosing
\varphi=e^{h\ell}(t;u)\in V^{h}
we derive from (
3.2.1b
) that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\,\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\,{\|e^{h\ell}(t;u)\|}_{H}^{2}+\gamma_{1}\,{\|e^{h\ell}(t;u)\|}_{V}^{2}-\gamma_{2}\|e^{h\ell}(t;u)\|_{H}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad\leq{\langle e^{h\ell}_{t}(t;u),e^{h\ell}(t;u)\rangle}_{V^{\prime},V}+a(t;e^{h\ell}(t;u),e^{h\ell}(t;u))

\displaystyle\quad=-{\langle r^{h\ell}(t;u),e^{h,\ell}(t;u)\rangle}_{V^{\prime},V}

Applying the Young inequality we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\,\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\,{\|e^{h\ell}(t;u)\|}_{H}^{2}+\gamma_{1}\,{\|e^{h\ell}(t;u)\|}_{V}^{2}-\gamma_{2}{\|e^{h\ell}(t;u)\|}_{H}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad\leq{\|r^{h\ell}(t;u)\|}_{(V^{h})^{\prime}}{\|e^{h\ell}(t;u)\|}_{V}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{1}{2\gamma_{1}}\,{\|r^{h\ell}(t;u)\|}_{(V^{h})^{\prime}}^{2}+\frac{\gamma_{1}}{2}\,{\|e^{h\ell}(t;u)\|}_{V}^{2},

which implies

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\,{\|e^{h\ell}(t;u)\|}_{H}^{2}+\gamma_{1}\,{\|e^{h\ell}(t;u)\|}_{V}^{2}-2\gamma_{2}{\|e^{h\ell}(t;u)\|}_{H}^{2}\leq\frac{1}{\gamma_{1}}\,{\|r^{h\ell}(t;u)\|}_{(V^{h})^{\prime}}^{2}.

(3.8.26)

From the embedding inequality (
2.4.1
), this yields

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\,{\|e^{h\ell}(t;u)\|}_{H}^{2}\leq c\|e^{h\ell}(t;u)\|_{H}^{2}+\frac{1}{\gamma_{1}}\,{\|r^{h\ell}(t;u)\|}_{(V^{h})^{\prime}}^{2}.

(3.8.27)

with the nonnegative constant
c=\max(2\gamma_{2}-\gamma_{1}/c_{V}^{2},0)
. From applying the Gronwall Lemma (Proposition
A.1.32
), it follows that

\displaystyle{\|e^{h\ell}(t;u)\|}_{H}^{2}\leq e^{ct}\bigg({\|e^{h\ell}(0;u)\|}_{H}^{2}+\frac{1}{\gamma_{1}}\int_{0}^{t}{\|r^{h\ell}(s;u)\|}_{(V^{h})^{\prime}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\bigg)

(3.8.28)

almost everywhere in
[0,T]
. Integrating (
3.8.26
) over
[0,T]
we find that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}{\|e^{h\ell}(t;u)\|}_{V}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{1}{\gamma_{1}}\bigg(\|e^{h\ell}(0;u)\|_{H}^{2}+\frac{1}{\gamma_{1}}\int_{0}^{T}\|r^{h\ell}(t;u)\|_{(V^{h})^{\prime}}^{2}~\mathrm{d}t+2\gamma_{2}\int_{0}^{T}\|e^{h\ell}(t;u)\|_{H}^{2}~\mathrm{d}t\bigg)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{1+2\gamma_{2}Te^{ct}}{\gamma_{1}}\bigg({\|e^{h\ell}(0;u)\|}_{H}^{2}+\frac{1}{\gamma_{1}}\int_{0}^{T}{\|r^{h\ell}(t;u)\|}_{(V^{h})^{\prime}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t\bigg),

where we have again used the estimate (
3.8.28
) in the integral after
\gamma_{2}
. Inserting
e^{h\ell}=y^{h}(\cdot\,;u)-y^{h\ell}(\cdot\,;u)
, using the initial conditions for
y^{h}(0)
and
y^{h\ell}(0)
we get the claim.
\Box

3.8.4
Proofs of Section
3.5

Proof of Theorem
3.5.2
.
Clearly,
y_{1}^{h}
is given by the projected initial condition
\mathcal{P}^{h}y_{\circ}\in V^{h}
. Furthermore,
y_{j}^{h}\in V^{h}
is is given for
j=2,\ldots,n
by (
3.5.3
) with the coordinate vectors
\mathrm{y}_{j\ell}^{h}
solving (
3.5.4a
). The matrix
{\bm{M}}^{h}+\delta t_{j}{\bm{A}}^{h}(t_{j})
is regular for sufficiently small
\Delta t
. Consequently, there exists a unique
y_{j}^{h}
. Next we derive the a-priori error bound for the solution to (
3.5.2
). We choose
\varphi^{h}=y_{j}^{h}\in V^{h}
. It follows from Lemma
A.2.41
(with
\varphi=y_{j-1}^{h}
and
\phi=y_{j}^{h}
) that

\displaystyle{\langle y_{j-1}^{h}-y_{j}^{h},y_{j}^{h}\rangle}_{H}=\frac{1}{2}\,{\|y_{j-1}^{h}\|}_{H}^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\,{\|y_{j}^{h}-y_{j-1}^{h}\|}_{H}^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\,{\|y_{j}^{h}\|}_{H}^{2}

which implies

\displaystyle{\langle y_{j}^{h}-y_{j-1}^{h},y_{j}^{h}\rangle}_{H}=\frac{1}{2}\,{\|y_{j}^{h}\|}_{H}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\,{\|y_{j}^{h}-y_{j-1}^{h}\|}_{H}^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\,{\|y_{j-1}^{h}\|}_{H}^{2}.

Plugging the last identity into (
3.5.2a
) and using (
3.2.1b
) yields

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\delta t_{j}}\,\big({\|y_{j}^{h}\|}_{H}^{2}-{\|y_{j-1}^{h}\|}_{H}^{2}\big)+\frac{1}{2\delta t_{j}}\,{\|y_{j}^{h}-y_{j-1}^{h}\|}_{H}^{2}+\gamma_{1}\,{\|y_{j}^{h}\|}_{V}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad\leq{\|g_{j}(u)\|}_{V^{\prime}}{\|y_{j}^{h}\|}_{V}\leq\frac{1}{2\gamma_{1}}\,{\|g_{j}(u)\|}^{2}_{V^{\prime}}+\frac{\gamma_{1}}{2}\,{\|y_{j}^{h}\|}_{V}^{2}.

Multiplication by
2\delta t_{j}
gives

{\|y_{j}^{h}\|}_{H}^{2}-{\|y_{j-1}^{h}\|}_{H}^{2}+{\|y_{j}^{h}-y_{j-1}^{h}\|}_{H}^{2}+\gamma_{1}\delta t_{j}\,{\|y_{j}^{h}\|}_{V}^{2}\leq\frac{\delta t_{j}}{\gamma_{1}}\,{\|g_{j}(u)\|}_{V^{\prime}}^{2}

(3.8.29)

for
j=2,\ldots,n
. From (
2.4.1
) and
\delta t_{j}\geq\delta t>0
it follows that

\big(1+c_{1}\delta t\big)\,{\|y_{j}^{h}\|}_{H}^{2}\leq{\|y_{j}^{h}\|}_{H}^{2}+\gamma_{1}\delta t_{j}\,{\|y_{j}^{h}\|}_{V}^{2}

(3.8.30)

with
c_{1}=\gamma_{1}/c_{V}^{2}
. Now, combining (
3.8.30
) and (
3.8.29
) yields after summation over
j=2,\ldots,n

\displaystyle{\|y_{j}^{h}\|}_{H}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{1+c_{1}\delta t}\,{\|y_{j-1}^{h}\|}_{H}^{2}+\frac{\delta t_{j}}{\gamma_{1}(1+c_{1}\delta t_{j})}\,{\|g_{j}(u)\|}_{V^{\prime}}^{2}

(3.8.31)

\displaystyle\leq\bigg(\frac{1}{1+c_{1}\delta t}\bigg)^{j-1}\bigg({\|y_{1}^{h}\|}_{H}^{2}+\frac{1}{\gamma_{1}}\sum_{l=2}^{j}\delta t_{l}\,{\|g_{l}(u)\|}_{V^{\prime}}^{2}\bigg).

Suppose that

\delta t\leq\frac{1}{2c_{1}}.

(3.8.32)

Then we infer
1+c_{1}\delta t\leq 1/2
and

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1+c_{1}\delta t}=1-\frac{c_{1}\delta t}{1+c_{1}\delta t}\leq 1-2c_{1}\delta t.

Due to (
3.8.32
) we also have
-2c_{1}\delta t>-1
such that

\bigg(\frac{1}{1+c_{1}\delta t}\bigg)^{j-1}\leq\big(1-2c_{1}\delta t\big)^{j-1}\leq e^{-c_{2}(j-1)\delta t}

(3.8.33)

with
c_{2}=2c_{1}
. Thus, (
3.5.2b
), (
3.8.31
), (
3.8.32
) and (
3.8.33
) imply

\displaystyle{\|y_{j}^{h}\|}_{H}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq e^{-c_{2}(j-1)\delta t}\bigg({\|y_{1}^{h}\|}_{H}^{2}+\frac{1}{\gamma_{1}}\sum_{l=2}^{j}\delta t_{l}\,{\|g_{l}(u)\|}_{V^{\prime}}^{2}\bigg)

(3.8.34)

\displaystyle\leq e^{-c_{2}(j-1)\delta t}\bigg({\|\mathcal{P}^{h}y_{\circ}\|}_{H}^{2}+\frac{1}{\gamma_{1}}\sum_{l=2}^{j}\delta t_{l}\,{\|g_{l}(u)\|}_{V^{\prime}}^{2}\bigg)

for
j=2,\ldots,n
, which gives (
3.5.5
) for
j=2,\ldots,n
. Summation upon
j
in (
3.8.29
) yields (
3.5.6
).
\Box

Proof of Theorem
3.5.3
.
Let
y
and
\{y^{h}_{j}\}_{j=1}^{n}
be the solutions to (
3.2.4
) and (
3.5.2
), respectively. It was assumed that both
y(t_{j})
and
y_{t}(t_{j})
belong to
\mathscr{W}
for
j=1,\ldots,n
. To estimate the error we proceed analogously as in the proof of Theorem
3.4.7
. Therefore, we introduce the decomposition

\displaystyle y(t_{j})-y^{h}_{j}=y(t_{j})-\mathcal{P}^{h}y(t_{j})+\mathcal{P}^{h}y(t_{j})-y_{j}^{h}=\varrho_{j}^{h}+\vartheta_{j}^{h}\quad\text{for }j=1,\ldots,n,

where we have set
\varrho_{j}^{h}=y(t_{j})-\mathcal{P}^{h}y(t_{j})\in V
and
\vartheta_{j}^{h}=\mathcal{P}^{h}y(t_{j})-y_{j}^{h}\in V^{h}
. From (
3.4.3
) it follows that there exist two constants
C_{1},C_{2}>0
satisfying

{\|\varrho_{j}^{h}\|}_{H}^{2}\leq C_{1}h^{4}\,{\|y(t_{j})\|}^{2}_{\mathscr{W}}\quad\text{and}\quad{\|\varrho_{j}^{h}\|}^{2}_{V}\leq C_{2}h^{2}\,{\|y(t_{j})\|}^{2}_{\mathscr{W}}

(3.8.35)

for
j=1,\ldots,n
. Moreover, (
3.4.3
) implies that

{\|y_{t}(t_{j})-\mathcal{P}^{h}y_{t}(t_{j})\|}_{H}\leq C_{1}h^{4}\,{\|y_{t}(t_{j})\|}^{2}_{\mathscr{W}}

(3.8.36)

for
j=1,\ldots,n
. We use the notations
\overline{\partial}\vartheta_{j}^{h}=(\vartheta_{j}^{h}-\vartheta_{j-1}^{h})/\delta t_{j}
and
\overline{\partial}y(t_{j})=(y(t_{j})-y(t_{j-1}))/\delta t_{j}
for
j=2,\ldots,n
. From (
3.2.4
), (
3.5.2
) and (
3.2.1a
) we derive for every
\varphi^{h}\in V

\displaystyle{\langle\overline{\partial}\vartheta_{j}^{h},\varphi^{h}\rangle}_{H}+a(t_{j};\vartheta_{j}^{h},\varphi^{h})

\displaystyle={\langle\mathcal{P}^{h}\overline{\partial}y(t_{j})-y_{t}(t_{j}),\varphi^{h}\rangle}_{H}+a(t_{j};\mathcal{P}^{h}y(t_{j})-y(t_{j}),\varphi^{h})

(3.8.37)

\displaystyle\leq{\|\mathcal{P}^{h}\overline{\partial}y(t_{j})-y_{t}(t_{j})\|}_{H}{\|\varphi^{h}\|}_{H}+\gamma\,{\|\varrho_{j}^{h}\|}_{V}{\|\varphi^{h}\|}_{V}.

Choosing
\varphi^{h}=\vartheta_{j}^{h}\in V^{h}
, using (
3.2.1b
), Young’s inequality and Lemma
A.2.41
we infer from (
3.8.37
) that

{\|\vartheta_{j}^{h}\|}_{H}^{2}+\gamma_{1}\delta t_{j}\,{\|\vartheta_{j}^{h}\|}_{V}^{2}\leq{\|\vartheta_{j-1}^{h}\|}_{H}^{2}+\delta t_{j}\left(\frac{2c_{V}^{2}}{\gamma_{1}}\,{\|\mathcal{P}^{h}\overline{\partial}y(t_{j})-y_{t}(t_{j})\|}_{H}^{2}+\frac{2\gamma^{2}}{\gamma_{1}}\,{\|\varrho_{j}^{h}\|}_{V}^{2}\right)

(3.8.38)

for
j=2,\ldots,n
, where the constant
c_{V}
has been introduced in (
2.4.1
). Note that

{\|y_{t}(t_{j})-\overline{\partial}y(t_{j})\|}_{H}\leq\frac{1}{\delta t_{j}}\bigg\|\int_{t_{j-1}}^{t_{j}}(t-t_{j-1})y_{tt}(t)\,\mathrm{d}t\bigg\|_{H}\leq\sqrt{\frac{\delta t_{j}}{3}}{\|y_{tt}\|}_{L^{2}(t_{j-1},t_{j};H)}.

(3.8.39)

From (
3.8.36
) and (
3.8.39
) we have

\displaystyle{\|\mathcal{P}^{h}\overline{\partial}y(t_{j})-\overline{\partial}y(t_{j})\|}_{H}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 3\,{\|\mathcal{P}^{h}\overline{\partial}y(t_{j})-\mathcal{P}^{h}y_{t}(t_{j})\|}_{H}^{2}+3\,{\|\mathcal{P}^{h}y_{t}(t_{j})-y_{t}(t_{j})\|}_{H}^{2}+3\,{\|y_{t}(t_{j})-\overline{\partial}y(t_{j})\|}_{H}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\big(1+{\|\mathcal{P}^{h}\|}_{\mathscr{L}(X,H)}^{2}\big)\delta t_{j}{\|y_{tt}\|}_{L^{2}(t_{j-1},t_{j};H)}^{2}+3\,C_{1}h^{4}\,{\|y_{t}(t_{j})\|}^{2}_{\mathscr{W}}

for
j=2,\ldots,n
. Hence, there is a constant
C_{3}>0
such that

{\|\mathcal{P}^{h}\overline{\partial}y(t_{j})-\overline{\partial}y(t_{j})\|}_{H}^{2}\leq C_{3}\,\big(\delta t_{j}\,{\|y_{tt}\|}_{L^{2}(t_{j-1},t_{j};H)}^{2}+h^{4}\,{\|y_{t}(t_{j})\|}^{2}_{\mathscr{W}}\big)

(3.8.40)

for
j=2,\ldots,n
. Thus, combining (
3.8.35
), (
3.8.38
), (
3.8.39
) and (
3.8.40
) yields

\displaystyle 6{\|\vartheta_{j}^{h}\|}_{H}^{2}+\gamma_{1}\delta t_{j}\,{\|\vartheta_{j}^{h}\|}_{V}^{2}

(3.8.41)

\displaystyle\leq{\|\vartheta_{j-1}^{h}\|}_{H}^{2}+C_{4}\delta t_{j}\,\big(\delta t_{j}\,{\|y_{tt}\|}_{L^{2}(t_{j-1},t_{j};H)}^{2}+h^{4}\,{\|y_{t}(t_{j})\|}^{2}_{\mathscr{W}}+h^{2}\,{\|y(t_{j})\|}^{2}_{\mathscr{W}}\big)

for
j=2,\ldots,n
and a suitable constant
C_{4}>0
. Further,
\vartheta_{1}^{h}=0
holds. From numerical integration it follows that

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\delta t_{i}\,\big(h^{2}\,{\|y_{t}(t_{i})\|}^{2}_{\mathscr{W}}+{\|y(t_{j})\|}^{2}_{\mathscr{W}}\big)\leq h^{2}\,{\|y_{t}\|}_{L^{2}(0,T;\mathscr{W})}^{2}+{\|y\|}_{L^{2}(0,T;\mathscr{W})}^{2}+C_{5}\Delta t^{2}

for a constant
C_{5}>0
provided
y_{tt}\in L^{2}(0,T;\mathscr{W})
holds. Summation of (
3.8.41
) over
i=2,\ldots,j
for
j\in\{2,\ldots,n\}
implies that

\displaystyle{\|\vartheta_{j}^{h}\|}_{H}^{2}+\gamma_{1}\,\sum_{i=1}^{j}\delta t_{i}{\|\vartheta_{j}^{h}\|}_{V}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{4}\,\bigg(\Delta t^{2}\,{\|y_{tt}\|}_{L^{2}(0,T;H)}^{2}+h^{2}\big(h^{2}\,{\|y\|}_{L^{2}(0,T;\mathscr{W})}^{2}+{\|y_{t}\|}_{L^{2}(0,T;\mathscr{W})}^{2}+C_{5}\Delta t^{2}\big)\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq C_{6}\,\big(\Delta t^{2}+h^{2}+\Delta t^{2}h^{2}\big)

for
j=2,\ldots,n
and for a constant
C_{6}>0
depending on
y,y_{t}
and
y_{tt}
. Thus, we have on the one hand

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{n}\delta t_{j}\,{\|y(t_{j})-y^{h}_{j}\|}_{H}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\sum_{j=1}^{n}\delta t_{j}\,{\|\varrho_{j}^{h}\|}_{H}^{2}+2\sum_{j=1}^{n}\delta t_{j}\,{\|\vartheta_{j}^{h}\|}_{H}^{2}\leq C_{7}\,\big(\Delta t^{2}+h^{2}+\Delta t^{2}h^{2}\big)

for a constant
C_{7}>0
and on the other hand with the same argument

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{n}\delta t_{j}\,{\|y(t_{j})-y^{h}_{j}\|}_{V}^{2}\leq C_{8}\,\big(\Delta t^{2}+h^{2}+\Delta t^{2}h^{2}\big).

for a constant
C_{8}>0
.
\Box

Proof of Lemma
3.5.8
.
We use the notation
\overline{\partial}\vartheta_{j}^{h\ell}=(\vartheta_{j}^{h\ell}-\vartheta_{j-1}^{h\ell})/\delta t_{j}
for
2\leq j\leq n
. From (
3.5.2
), (
3.5.14
), (
3.2.1a
) and
\varrho_{j}^{h\ell}=y^{h}_{j}-\mathcal{P}^{h\ell}y^{h}_{j}
for
j=1,\ldots,n
, we obtain for every
\psi\in V

\displaystyle{\langle\overline{\partial}\vartheta_{j}^{h\ell},\psi\rangle}_{H}+a(t_{j};\vartheta_{j}^{h\ell},\psi)

\displaystyle={\langle\overline{\partial}\mathcal{P}^{h\ell}y^{h}_{j},\psi\rangle}_{H}+a(t_{j};\mathcal{P}^{h\ell}y^{h}_{j},\psi)-{\langle\overline{\partial}y^{h\ell}_{j},\psi\rangle}_{H}-a(t_{j};y^{h\ell}_{j},\psi)

(3.8.42)

\displaystyle={\langle\mathcal{P}^{h\ell}\overline{\partial}y^{h}_{j}-\overline{\partial}y^{h}_{j},\psi\rangle}_{H}+a(t_{j};\mathcal{P}^{h\ell}y^{h}_{j}-y^{h}_{j},\psi)

\displaystyle\leq{\|\overline{\partial}\varrho_{j}^{h\ell}\|}_{H}{\|\psi\|}_{H}+\gamma\,{\|\varrho_{j}^{h\ell}\|}_{V}{\|\psi\|}_{V}\quad\text{for }2\leq j\leq n.

Let us choose
\psi=\vartheta_{j}^{h\ell}
in (
3.8.42
). Utilizing (
3.2.1b
), Lemma
A.2.41
and the Young inequality we get

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\|\vartheta_{j}^{h\ell}\|}_{H}^{2}-{\|\vartheta_{j-1}^{h\ell}\|}_{H}^{2}+{\|\vartheta_{j}^{h\ell}-\vartheta_{j-1}^{h\ell}\|}_{H}^{2}+2\gamma_{1}\delta t_{j}\,{\|\vartheta_{j}^{h\ell}\|}_{V}^{2}

(3.8.43)

\displaystyle\leq\delta t_{j}\bigg(\frac{2c_{V}^{2}}{\gamma_{1}}\,{\|\overline{\partial}\varrho_{j}^{h\ell}\|}_{H}^{2}+\frac{\gamma_{1}}{2c_{V}^{2}}\,{\|\vartheta_{j}^{h\ell}\|}_{H}^{2}+\frac{\gamma_{1}}{2}\,{\|\vartheta_{j}^{h\ell}\|}_{V}^{2}+\frac{2\gamma^{2}}{\gamma_{1}}\,{\|\varrho_{j}^{h\ell}\|}_{V}^{2}\bigg).

From (
2.4.1
) and (
3.8.43
) we find

\displaystyle{\|\vartheta_{j}^{h\ell}\|}_{H}^{2}\leq{\|\vartheta_{j-1}^{h\ell}\|}_{H}^{2}+c_{1}\delta t_{j}\big({\|\varrho_{j}^{h\ell}\|}_{V}^{2}+{\|\overline{\partial}\varrho_{j}^{h\ell}\|}_{V}^{2}\big)

with
c_{1}=2\max(c_{V}^{4},\gamma^{2})/\gamma_{1}
. Recall that
\vartheta_{1}^{h\ell}=0
. By summation on
j
we find

\displaystyle{\|\vartheta_{j}^{h\ell}\|}_{H}^{2}\leq c_{1}\sum_{l=2}^{j}\delta t_{l}\left({\|\varrho_{l}^{h\ell}\|}_{V}^{2}+{\|\overline{\partial}\varrho_{l}^{h\ell}\|}_{V}^{2}\right)\leq c_{1}\sum_{l=1}^{n}\delta t_{l}\left({\|\varrho_{l}^{h\ell}\|}_{V}^{2}+{\|\overline{\partial}\varrho_{l}^{h\ell}\|}_{V}^{2}\right).

Using
\delta t_{l}\leq 2\alpha_{l}^{n}
and (
3.5.19
) we have

\displaystyle{\|\vartheta_{j}^{h\ell}\|}_{H}^{2}\leq 2c_{1}\sum_{l=1}^{n}\alpha_{l}^{n}\left({\|\varrho_{l}^{h\ell}\|}_{V}^{2}+{\|\overline{\partial}\varrho_{l}^{h\ell}\|}_{V}^{2}\right)=2c_{1}\sum_{k=1}^{2}\sum_{l=1}^{n}\alpha_{l}^{n}\,{\|y_{l}^{k}-\mathcal{P}^{h\ell}y_{l}^{k}\|}_{V}^{2}.

Recall that
\sum_{j=1}^{n}\alpha_{j}=T
. Consequently,

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{n}\alpha_{j}^{n}\,{\|\vartheta_{j}^{h\ell}\|}_{H}^{2}\leq 2c_{1}T\sum_{k=1}^{2}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\alpha_{j}^{n}\,{\|y_{j}^{k}-\mathcal{P}^{h\ell}y_{j}^{k}\|}_{V}^{2}.

Setting
c_{2}=2(c_{V}^{2}+2c_{1}T)
and using (
2.4.1
), (
3.5.19
) we find that

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{n}\alpha_{j}^{n}\,{\|y^{h}_{j}-y^{h\ell}_{j}\|}_{H}^{2}\leq 2\sum_{j=1}^{n}\alpha_{j}^{n}\,{\|\varrho^{h\ell}_{j}\|}_{H}^{2}+2\sum_{j=1}^{n}\alpha_{j}^{n}\,{\|\vartheta^{h\ell}_{j}\|}_{H}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad\leq 2c_{V}^{2}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\alpha_{j}^{n}\,{\|\varrho^{h\ell}_{j}\|}_{V}^{2}+4c_{1}T\sum_{k=1}^{2}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\alpha_{j}^{n}\,{\|y_{j}^{k}-\mathcal{P}^{h\ell}y_{j}^{k}\|}_{V}^{2}\leq c_{2}\sum_{k=1}^{2}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\alpha_{j}^{n}\,{\|y_{j}^{k}-\mathcal{P}^{h\ell}y_{j}^{k}\|}_{V}^{2}.

From (
3.5.8
), (
3.8.43
) and
\vartheta_{1}^{h\ell}=0
we also derive

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{n}\alpha_{j}^{n}\,{\|\vartheta_{j}^{h\ell}\|}_{V}^{2}\leq\zeta\sum_{j=1}^{n}\delta t_{j}\,{\|\vartheta_{j}^{h\ell}\|}_{V}^{2}=\zeta\sum_{j=2}^{n}\delta t_{j}\,{\|\vartheta_{j}^{h\ell}\|}_{V}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{c_{1}\zeta}{\gamma_{1}}\sum_{j=2}^{n}\delta t_{j}\,\left({\|\overline{\partial}\varrho_{j}^{h\ell}\|}_{V}^{2}+{\|\varrho_{j}^{h\ell}\|}_{V}^{2}\right)\leq C\sum_{k=1}^{2}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\alpha_{j}^{n}\,{\|y_{j}^{k}-\mathcal{P}^{h\ell}y_{j}^{k}\|}_{V}^{2}

which gives (
3.5.21
) with
C=2c_{1}\zeta/\gamma_{1}
.
\Box

Proof of Theorem
3.5.11
.
Let
\psi\in X^{n\ell}
. Then
\mathcal{P}^{n\ell}\psi=\psi
holds and using (
3.8.19
) we find

{\langle\mathcal{P}^{n\ell}\overline{\partial}y_{j}^{h},\psi\rangle}_{H}=\frac{1}{\delta t_{j}}\,{\langle\mathcal{P}^{n\ell}(y^{h}_{j}-y_{j-1}^{h}),\psi\rangle}_{H}=\frac{1}{\delta t_{j}}\,{\langle y_{j}^{h}-y_{j-1}^{h},\mathcal{P}^{n\ell}\psi\rangle}_{H}={\langle\overline{\partial}y_{j}^{h},\psi\rangle}_{H}

(3.8.44)

for
j=2,\ldots,n
. Thus, we can modify the estimation of
\vartheta_{j}^{h\ell}
. Instead of (
3.8.42
) we find that for any
\psi\in X^{n\ell}

\displaystyle{\langle\overline{\partial}\vartheta_{j}^{h\ell},\psi\rangle}_{H}+a(t_{j};\vartheta_{j}^{h\ell},\psi)

\displaystyle={\langle\mathcal{P}^{n\ell}\overline{\partial}y^{h}_{j}-\overline{\partial}y^{h}_{j},\psi\rangle}_{H}+a(t_{j};\mathcal{P}^{n\ell}y^{h}_{j}-y^{h\ell}_{j},\psi)

\displaystyle=a(t_{j};\mathcal{P}^{n\ell}y^{h}_{j}-y^{h\ell}_{j},\psi)\leq\gamma\,{\|\varrho_{j}^{h\ell}\|}_{V}{\|\psi\|}_{V}

for
j=2,\ldots,n
. Thus, we derive instead of (
3.8.43
)

\displaystyle{\|\vartheta_{j}^{h\ell}\|}_{H}^{2}-{\|\vartheta_{j-1}^{h\ell}\|}_{H}^{2}+{\|\vartheta_{j}^{h\ell}-\vartheta_{j-1}^{h\ell}\|}_{H}^{2}+2\gamma_{1}\delta t_{j}\,{\|\vartheta_{j}^{h\ell}\|}_{V}^{2}\leq\delta t_{j}\bigg(\gamma_{1}\,{\|\vartheta_{j}^{h\ell}\|}_{V}^{2}+\frac{\gamma^{2}}{\gamma_{1}}\,{\|\varrho_{j}^{h\ell}\|}_{V}^{2}\bigg)

for
j=2,\ldots,n
. Consequently,

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{n}\alpha_{j}^{n}\,{\|\vartheta_{j}^{h\ell}\|}_{H}^{2}\leq\frac{\gamma^{2}T}{\gamma_{1}}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\alpha_{j}^{n}\,{\|y_{j}^{1}-\mathcal{P}^{n\ell}y_{j}^{1}\|}_{V}^{2}

and

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{n}\alpha_{j}^{n}\,{\|\vartheta_{j}^{h\ell}\|}_{V}^{2}\leq\zeta\sum_{j=2}^{n}\delta t_{j}\,{\|\vartheta_{j}^{h\ell}\|}_{V}^{2}\leq c_{4}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\alpha_{j}^{n}\,{\|y_{j}^{1}-\mathcal{P}^{n\ell}y_{j}^{1}\|}_{V}^{2}

with
c_{4}=2\zeta\gamma^{2}/\gamma_{1}^{2}
. Now we argue as in the proof of Theorem
3.5.9
.
\Box

Proof of Theorem
3.5.12
.
We set
e_{j}^{h\ell}=y^{h}_{j}-y^{h\ell}_{j}\in V^{h}
for
j=1,\ldots,n
and
\overline{\partial}e_{j}^{h\ell}=(e_{j}^{h\ell}-e_{j-1}^{h\ell})/\delta t_{j}\in V^{h}
for
j=2,\ldots,n
. Then we conclude that for every
\varphi^{h}\in V^{h}
we have

\displaystyle{\langle\overline{\partial}e_{j}^{h\ell},\varphi^{h}\rangle}_{H}+a(t_{j};e_{j}^{h\ell},\varphi^{h})={\langle\overline{\partial}y_{j}^{h}-\overline{\partial}y_{j}^{h\ell},\varphi^{h}\rangle}_{H}+a(t_{j};y^{h}_{j}-y^{h\ell}_{j},\varphi^{h})

\displaystyle\quad={\langle(\mathcal{F}+\mathcal{B}u)(t_{j}),\varphi^{h}\rangle}_{V^{\prime},V}-{\langle\overline{\partial}y_{j}^{h\ell},\varphi^{h}\rangle}_{H}-a(t_{j};y^{h\ell}_{j},\varphi^{h})=-{\langle r_{j}^{h\ell},\varphi^{h}\rangle}_{(V^{h})^{\prime},V^{h}}

for
j=2,\ldots,n
with the residual
r_{j}^{h\ell}
given by (
3.5.22
) for
j=2,\ldots,n
. Choosing
\varphi^{h}=e_{j}^{h\ell}\in V^{h}
and using
\|e_{j}^{h\ell}\|_{V^{h}}=\|e_{j}^{h\ell}\|_{V}
for
\varphi\in V^{h}
we find

\displaystyle{\langle\overline{\partial}e_{j}^{h\ell},e_{j}^{h\ell}\rangle}_{H}+a(t_{j};e_{j}^{h\ell},e_{j}^{h\ell})\leq{\|r_{j}^{h\ell}\|}_{(V^{h})^{\prime}}{\|e_{j}^{h\ell}\|}_{V}

for
j=2,\ldots,n
. From (
3.2.4b
), Lemma
A.2.4
and Young’s inequality we infer that

\displaystyle{\|e_{j}^{h\ell}\|}_{H}^{2}-{\|e_{j-1}^{h\ell}\|}_{H}^{2}+{\|e_{j}^{h\ell}-e_{j-1}^{h\ell}\|}_{H}^{2}+2\gamma_{1}\delta t_{j}\,{\|e_{j}^{h\ell}\|}_{V}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{\delta t_{j}}{\gamma_{1}}\,{\|r_{j}^{h\ell}\|}_{(V^{h})^{\prime}}^{2}+\gamma_{1}\delta t_{j}\,{\|e_{j}^{h\ell}\|}_{V}^{2}\quad\text{for }2\leq j\leq n.

This yields

{\|e_{j}^{h\ell}\|}_{H}^{2}+\gamma_{1}\delta t_{j}\,{\|e_{j}^{h\ell}\|}_{V}^{2}\leq{\|e_{j-1}^{h\ell}\|}_{H}^{2}+\frac{\delta t_{j}}{\gamma_{1}}\,{\|r_{j}^{h\ell}\|}_{(V^{h})^{\prime}}^{2}\quad\text{for }2\leq j\leq n.

(3.8.45)

Using (
2.4.1
) and setting
c_{1}=\gamma_{1}/c_{V}^{2}
it follows from (
3.8.45
) that

\displaystyle{\|e_{j}^{h\ell}\|}_{H}^{2}\leq\frac{1}{1+c_{1}\delta t_{j}}\,{\|e_{j-1}^{h\ell}\|}_{H}^{2}+\frac{\delta t_{j}}{\gamma_{1}(1+c_{1}\delta t_{j})}\,{\|r_{j}^{h\ell}\|}_{(V^{h})^{\prime}}^{2}\quad\text{for }2\leq j\leq n.

From
e_{1}^{h\ell}=0
, (
3.8.32
), (
3.8.33
) and by summation upon
j
we derive

\displaystyle{\|e_{j}^{h\ell}\|}_{H}^{2}\leq\frac{e^{-c_{2}(j-1)\delta t}}{\gamma_{1}}\Delta t\sum_{l=2}^{j}\,{\|r_{l}^{h\ell}\|}_{(V^{h})^{\prime}}^{2}\quad\text{for }2\leq j\leq n.

with
c_{2}=2c_{1}
. Arguing as in (
3.5.21
), using again
e_{1}^{h\ell}=0
and also (
3.8.45
) the second estimate follows.
\Box

3.8.5
Proofs of Section
3.6

Proof of Proposition
3.6.5
.
Let us insert
\psi=y^{\ell}(t)
into the Galerkin system (
3.6.9
), use Assumption
3.6.1
and apply Young’s inequality to get for almost all
t\in[0,T]

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\,\|y^{\ell}(t)\|_{H}^{2}+\gamma_{1}\|y^{\ell}(t)\|_{V}^{2}-\gamma_{2}\|y^{\ell}(t)\|_{H}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|(\mathcal{F}+\mathcal{B}u)(t)\|_{V^{\prime}}\|y^{\ell}(t)\|_{V}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\gamma_{1}}{2}\|y^{\ell}(t)\|_{V}^{2}+\frac{1}{2\gamma_{1}}\|(\mathcal{F}+\mathcal{B}u)(t)\|_{V^{\prime}}^{2}.

Consequently, we obtain (
3.8.1
), so that we can proceed as in the proof of Theorem
3.2.4
to get a bound for
\|y^{\ell}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;V)}
; cf. (
3.8.4
). To estimate
\|y_{t}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;V^{\prime})}
we argue as in the proof of Theorem
3.3.5
; cf. (
3.8.9
). Hence, we obtain (
3.6.10
). Next we prove the uniqueness of the solution. Suppose that
\hat{y}^{\ell}
and
\tilde{y}^{\ell}
are two solutions to (
3.6.9
). Then
y^{\ell}=\hat{y}^{\ell}-\tilde{y}^{\ell}
satisfies

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\,{\langle y^{\ell}(t),\psi\rangle}_{H}+a(y^{\ell}(t),\varphi)+{\langle\mathcal{C}y^{\ell}(t),\psi\rangle}_{V^{\prime},V}\quad=-\langle\mathcal{N}(\hat{y}^{\ell}(t))-\mathcal{N}(\tilde{y}^{\ell}(t)),\psi\rangle_{V^{\prime},V}

for all
\psi\in X^{\ell}
a.e. in
(0,T]
. Choosing
\psi=y^{\ell}(t)\in X^{\ell}
for almost all
t\in[0,T]
, utilizing (
3.6.3
), (
3.6.5
) and Young’s inequality, it follows that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\,\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\,{\|y^{\ell}(t)\|}_{H}^{2}+\gamma_{1}\,{\|y^{\ell}(t)\|}_{V}^{2}\leq c_{\mathcal{M}}\,{\|\tilde{y}^{\ell}(t)\|}_{V}{\|y^{\ell}(t)\|}_{H}{\|y^{\ell}(t)\|}_{V}\leq\frac{\gamma_{1}}{2}\,{\|y^{\ell}(t)\|}^{2}_{V}+\frac{c_{\mathcal{M}}^{2}}{2\gamma_{1}}\,{\|\tilde{y}^{\ell}(t)\|}^{2}_{V}{\|y^{\ell}(t)\|}^{2}_{H}

for almost all
t\in[0,T]
. This especially implies

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\,{\|y^{\ell}(t)\|}_{H}^{2}\leq\Big(2\gamma_{2}+\frac{c_{\mathcal{M}}^{2}}{\gamma_{1}}\,{\|\tilde{y}^{\ell}(t)\|}_{V}^{2}\Big){\|y^{\ell}(t)\|}^{2}_{H}\quad\text{f.a.a. }t\in[0,T].

(3.8.46)

By the Gronwall lemma (cf. Proposition
A.1.32
) and
y^{\ell}(0)=\hat{y}(0)-\tilde{y}(0)=y_{\circ}-y_{\circ}=0
we obtain
\|y^{\ell}(t)\|_{H}^{2}\leq 0
for almost all
t\in[0,T]
. Thus,
\hat{y}^{\ell}(t)=\tilde{y}^{\ell}(t)
holds true for almost all
t\in[0,T]
.
\Box

Proof of Theorem
3.6.6
.
Recall that
X^{\ell}=\mathrm{span}\,\{\psi_{1},\ldots,\psi_{\ell}\}\subset V
for the two choices
X=H
and
X=V
. Again we shall use the decomposition

y(t)-y^{\ell}(t)=y(t)-\mathcal{P}^{\ell}y(t)+\mathcal{P}^{\ell}y(t)-y^{\ell}(t)=\varrho^{\ell}(t)+\vartheta^{\ell}(t),

(3.8.47)

where
\varrho^{\ell}(t)=y(t)-\mathcal{P}^{\ell}y(t)
and
\vartheta^{\ell}(t)=\mathcal{P}^{\ell}y(t)-y^{\ell}(t)\in X^{\ell}
. It follows that

\int_{0}^{T}{\|y(t)-y^{\ell}(t)\|}^{2}_{V}\,\mathrm{d}t\leq 2\int_{0}^{T}{\|\varrho^{\ell}(t)\|}^{2}_{V}\,\mathrm{d}t+2\int_{0}^{T}{\|\vartheta^{\ell}(t)\|}^{2}_{V}\,\mathrm{d}t.

(3.8.48)

First we estimate the
\vartheta^{\ell}
-term. Since
\mathcal{P}^{\ell}
is an
H
-orthonormal projection either for
X=H
or for
X=V
, we can apply (
3.8.20
). From (
3.6.1
), (
3.6.9
), (
3.6.2
) and
\varrho^{\ell}(t)=y(t)-\mathcal{P}^{\ell}y(t)
it follows that

\displaystyle{\langle\vartheta_{t}^{\ell}(t),\psi\rangle}_{H}+a(\vartheta^{\ell}(t),\psi)+{\langle\mathcal{C}\vartheta^{\ell}(t),\psi\rangle}_{V^{\prime},V}

\displaystyle\quad={\langle y_{t}(t),\psi\rangle}_{H}+a(t;\mathcal{P}^{\ell}y(t),\psi)+{\langle\mathcal{C}\mathcal{P}^{\ell}y(t),\psi\rangle}_{V^{\prime},V}-{\langle y_{t}^{\ell}(t),\psi\rangle}_{H}-a(t;y^{\ell}(t),\psi)-{\langle\mathcal{C}y^{\ell}(t),\psi\rangle}_{V^{\prime},V}

\displaystyle\quad=a((\mathcal{P}^{\ell}y-y)(t),\psi)+{\langle\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{P}^{\ell}y-y)(t),\psi\rangle}_{V^{\prime},V}+{\langle\mathcal{N}(y^{\ell}(t))-\mathcal{N}(y(t)),\psi\rangle}_{V^{\prime},V}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\gamma\,{\|\varrho^{\ell}(t)\|}_{V}{\|\psi\|}_{V}+{\|\mathcal{C}\varrho^{\ell}(t)\|}_{V^{\prime}}{\|\psi\|}_{V}+\big|{\langle\mathcal{N}(y^{\ell}(t))-\mathcal{N}(y(t)),\psi\rangle}_{V^{\prime},V}\big|.

Thus, choosing
\psi=\vartheta^{\ell}(t)\in X^{\ell}
and using (
3.6.3
) we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\,{\|\vartheta^{\ell}(t)\|}^{2}_{H}+\gamma_{1}\,{\|\vartheta^{\ell}(t)\|}_{V}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\gamma\,{\|\varrho^{\ell}(t)\|}_{V}{\|\vartheta^{\ell}(t)\|}_{V}+{\|\mathcal{C}\varrho^{\ell}(t)\|}_{V^{\prime}}{\|\vartheta^{\ell}(t)\|}_{V}

(3.8.49)

\displaystyle+\big|{\langle\mathcal{N}(y^{\ell}(t))-\mathcal{N}(y(t)),\vartheta^{\ell}(t)\rangle}_{V^{\prime},V}\big|.

Applying Young’s inequality it follows that

\gamma\,{\|\varrho^{\ell}(t)\|}_{V}{\|\vartheta^{\ell}(t)\|}_{V}\leq\frac{\gamma_{1}}{10}\,{\|\vartheta^{\ell}(t)\|}_{V}^{2}+c_{0}\,{\|\varrho^{\ell}(t)\|}_{V}^{2}

(3.8.50)

and

\displaystyle{\|\mathcal{C}\varrho^{\ell}(t)\|}_{V^{\prime}}{\|\vartheta^{\ell}(t)\|}_{V}

\displaystyle\leq{\|\mathcal{C}\|}_{\mathscr{L}(V,V^{\prime})}\,{\|\varrho^{\ell}(t)\|}_{V}{\|\vartheta^{\ell}(t)\|}_{V}

(3.8.51)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\gamma_{1}}{10}\,{\|\vartheta^{\ell}(t)\|}_{V}^{2}+c_{0}\,{\|\varrho^{\ell}(t)\|}_{V}^{2}

for a constant
c_{0}>0
depending on
\|\mathcal{C}\|_{\mathscr{L}(V,V^{\prime})}
,
\gamma
and
\gamma_{1}
. We proceed by estimating the nonlinear terms on the right-hand side of (
3.8.49
). Note that

\mathcal{N}(y^{\ell}(t))-\mathcal{N}(y(t))=\mathcal{M}(y(t),y^{\ell}(t)-y(t))+\mathcal{N}(y^{\ell}(t)-y(t))+\mathcal{M}(y^{\ell}(t)-y(t),y(t)).

(3.8.52)

Applying (
3.6.5
), (
2.4.1
), and Young’s inequality (with
p=q=2
and
\varepsilon=2\gamma_{1}/5
) we obtain satisfying

\displaystyle\big|{\langle\mathcal{M}(y(t),y^{\ell}(t)-y(t)),\vartheta^{\ell}(t)\rangle}_{V^{\prime},V}\big|

\displaystyle=\big|{\langle\mathcal{M}(y(t),\varrho^{\ell}(t)),\vartheta^{\ell}(t)\rangle}_{V^{\prime},V}\big|

(3.8.53)

\displaystyle\leq c_{\mathcal{M}}c_{V}^{\delta_{3}}\,{\|y\|}_{C([0,T];V)}{\|\varrho^{\ell}(t)\|}_{V}{\|\vartheta^{\ell}(t)\|}_{V}^{\delta_{3}}{\|\vartheta^{\ell}(t)\|}_{H}^{1-\delta_{3}}

\displaystyle\leq c_{1}\,{\|\varrho^{\ell}(t)\|}_{V}^{2}+\frac{\gamma_{1}}{5}\,{\|\vartheta^{\ell}(t)\|}_{V}^{2\delta_{3}}{\|\vartheta^{\ell}(t)\|}_{H}^{2(1-\delta_{3})}

with
c_{1}=5c_{\mathcal{M}}^{2}c_{V}^{2\delta_{3}}\,\|y\|_{C([0,T];V)}^{2}/(4\gamma_{1})
. Again applying Young’s inequality (with
p=1/\delta_{3}
,
q=1/(1-\delta_{3})
and
\varepsilon=1/(2\delta_{3})
) we find

{\|\vartheta^{\ell}(t)\|}_{V}^{2\delta_{3}}{\|\vartheta^{\ell}(t)\|}_{H}^{2(1-\delta_{3})}\leq\frac{1}{2}\,{\|\vartheta^{\ell}(t)\|}_{V}^{2}+\frac{1}{c_{2}}\,{\|\vartheta^{\ell}(t)\|}_{H}^{2}+c_{1}\,{\|\varrho^{\ell}(t)\|}_{V}^{2}

(3.8.54)

with
c_{2}=(\delta_{3}/2)^{\delta_{3}/(1-\delta_{3})}/(1-\delta_{3})
. Combining (
3.8.53
) and (
3.8.54
) we derive that

\displaystyle\big|{\langle\mathcal{M}(y(t),y^{\ell}(t)-y(t)),\vartheta^{\ell}(t)\rangle}_{V^{\prime},V}\big|

\displaystyle\leq c_{1}\,{\|\varrho^{\ell}(t)\|}_{V}^{2}+\frac{\gamma_{1}}{5}\,\bigg(\frac{1}{2}\,{\|\vartheta^{\ell}(t)\|}_{V}^{2}+\frac{1}{c_{2}}\,{\|\vartheta^{\ell}(t)\|}_{H}^{2}\bigg)

(3.8.55)

\displaystyle=\frac{\gamma_{1}}{10}\,{\|\vartheta^{\ell}(t)\|}_{V}^{2}+\frac{\gamma_{1}}{5c_{2}}\,{\|\vartheta^{\ell}(t)\|}_{H}^{2}+c_{1}\,{\|\varrho^{\ell}(t)\|}_{V}^{2}.

Again utilizing (
3.6.5
), Young’s inequality and (
2.4.1
) we find that there exist constants
c_{3},c_{4}>0
such that

\displaystyle\big|{\langle\mathcal{M}(y^{\ell}(t)-y(t),y(t)),\vartheta^{\ell}(t)\rangle}_{V^{\prime},V}\big|

(3.8.56)

\displaystyle=\big|{\langle\mathcal{M}(\vartheta^{\ell}(t),y(t))+\mathcal{M}(\varrho^{\ell}(t),y(t)),\vartheta^{\ell}(t)\rangle}_{V^{\prime},V}\big|

\displaystyle\leq c_{\mathcal{M}}\,{\|y\|}_{C([0,T];V)}\left({\|\vartheta^{\ell}(t)\|}_{H}{\|\vartheta^{\ell}(t)\|}_{V}+c_{V}^{\delta_{3}}\,{\|\varrho^{\ell}(t)\|}_{V}{\|\vartheta^{\ell}(t)\|}_{H}^{1-\delta_{3}}\,{\|\vartheta^{\ell}(t)\|}_{V}^{\delta_{3}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\gamma_{1}}{10}\,{\|\vartheta^{\ell}(t)\|}_{V}^{2}+c_{3}\,{\|\vartheta^{\ell}(t)\|}_{H}^{2}+c_{4}\,{\|\varrho^{\ell}(t)\|}_{V}^{2}.

By assumption,
y
belongs to
L^{\infty}(0,T;V)
. Due to (
2.4.1
) we have

\displaystyle{\|\varrho^{\ell}(t)\|}_{H}\leq c_{V}\,{\|y(t)-\mathcal{P}^{\ell}y(t)\|}_{V}\leq c_{V}\,{\|y(t)\|}_{V}\quad\text{f.a.a. }t\in[0,T].

Therefore, there exists a constant
c_{5}>0
such that

\esssup_{t\in[0,T]}\Big({\|\varrho^{\ell}(t)\|}_{H}^{\delta_{3}}{\|\varrho^{\ell}(t)\|}_{V}^{1-\delta_{3}},{\|\varrho^{\ell}(t)\|}_{V}\Big)\leq c_{5}.

(3.8.57)

Using (
3.8.47
) and (
3.6.5
) we conclude

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\langle\mathcal{N}(y^{\ell}(t)-y(t)),\vartheta^{\ell}(t)\rangle}_{V^{\prime},V}

(3.8.58)

\displaystyle=-{\langle\mathcal{M}(\vartheta^{\ell}(t),\varrho^{\ell}(t))-\mathcal{M}(\varrho^{\ell}(t),\varrho^{\ell}(t)),\vartheta^{\ell}(t)\rangle}_{V^{\prime},V}.

Applying (
3.6.3
), (
3.8.57
), (
3.8.58
) and Young’s inequality we find

\displaystyle\big|{\langle\mathcal{N}(y^{\ell}(t)-y(t)),\vartheta^{\ell}(t)\rangle}_{V^{\prime},V}\big|

(3.8.59)

\displaystyle\leq c_{\mathcal{M}}c_{5}\,\Big({\|\vartheta^{\ell}(t)\|}_{H}{\|\vartheta^{\ell}(t)\|}_{V}+{\|\varrho^{\ell}(t)\|}_{V}{\|\vartheta^{\ell}(t)\|}_{H}^{1-\delta_{3}}{\|\vartheta^{\ell}(t)\|}_{V}^{\delta_{3}}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\gamma_{1}}{10}\,{\|\vartheta^{\ell}(t)\|}_{V}^{2}+c_{6}\,{\|\vartheta^{\ell}(t)\|}_{H}^{2}+c_{7}\,{\|\varrho^{\ell}(t)\|}_{V}^{2}

for two constants
c_{6},c_{7}>0
. From (
3.8.49
)-(
3.8.59
)

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\,{\|\vartheta^{\ell}(t)\|}^{2}_{H}+\frac{\gamma_{1}}{2}\,{\|\vartheta^{\ell}(t)\|}_{V}^{2}\leq c_{8}\,\,{\|\vartheta^{\ell}(t)\|}_{H}^{2}+c_{9}\,{\|\varrho^{\ell}(t)\|}_{V}^{2},

(3.8.60)

where
c_{8}=c_{1}+c_{3}+c_{6}
and
c_{9}=2c_{0}+c_{2}+c_{4}+c_{7}
. Let

\displaystyle c_{10}=\left\{\begin{aligned} &0&&\text{if }1-\frac{\gamma_{1}}{2c_{V}^{2}}\leq 0,\\
&1-\frac{\gamma_{1}}{2c_{V}^{2}}&&\text{otherwise}\end{aligned}\right.

Thus, from
\vartheta^{\ell}(0)=0
, (
2.4.1
), (
3.8.60
) and Proposition
A.1.32
we obtain

{\|\vartheta^{\ell}(t)\|}^{2}_{H}\leq c_{11}\int_{0}^{t}{\|y(s)-\mathcal{P}^{\ell}y(s)\|}_{V}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\quad\text{in }[0,T]\text{ a.e.},

(3.8.61)

for
c_{11}=c_{12}\exp(c_{10}T)
and
c_{12}=\max(c_{8},c_{9})
. Thus,

\int_{0}^{T}{\|\vartheta^{\ell}(t)\|}^{2}_{H}\,\mathrm{d}t\leq c_{13}\int_{0}^{T}{\|y(t)-\mathcal{P}^{\ell}y(t)\|}_{V}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t\quad\text{in }[0,T]\text{ a.e.}

(3.8.62)

with
c_{13}=c_{11}T
. Integrating (
3.8.60
) over
[0,T]
and using
\vartheta^{\ell}(0)=0
as well as (
3.8.61
) it follows that

\int_{0}^{T}{\|\vartheta^{\ell}(t)\|}^{2}_{V}\,\mathrm{d}t\leq\frac{2}{\gamma_{1}}\int_{0}^{T}c_{8}\,{\|\vartheta^{\ell}(t)\|}_{H}^{2}+c_{9}\,{\|\varrho^{\ell}(t)\|}_{V}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t\leq c_{14}\int_{0}^{T}{\|y(t)-\mathcal{P}^{\ell}y(t)\|}_{V}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t

(3.8.63)

with
c_{14}=2\max(c_{8}c_{11}T,c_{9})/\gamma_{1}
. Thus, we find from (
3.8.48
) that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}{\|y(t)-y^{\ell}(t)\|}_{V}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t\leq 2\big(1+c_{14}\big)\int_{0}^{T}{\|y(t)-\mathcal{P}^{\ell}y(t)\|}_{V}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t

for the
H
-orthogonal projection operators
\mathcal{P}^{\ell}=\mathcal{P}^{\ell}_{H}
and
\mathcal{P}^{\ell}=\mathcal{Q}^{\ell}_{H}
. From Proposition
2.4.10
we finally infer the a-priori error estimate.
\Box

Proof of Theorem
3.6.8
.
For the a-priori error analysis we follow the proof of Theorem
3.6.6
. Thus, we make use of the following decomposition

\displaystyle y^{h}(t)-y^{h\ell}(t)

\displaystyle=y^{h}(t)-\mathcal{P}^{\ell}y^{h}(t)+\mathcal{P}^{\ell}y^{h}(t)-y^{h\ell}(t)=\varrho^{h\ell}(t)+\vartheta^{h\ell}(t)\quad\text{in }[0,T]\text{ a.e.}

with
\varrho^{h\ell}(t)=y^{h}(t)-\mathcal{P}^{\ell}y^{h}(t)\in(X^{\ell})^{\bot}
and
\vartheta^{h\ell}(t)=\mathcal{P}^{\ell}y^{h}(t)-y^{h\ell}(t)\in X^{\ell}
. It is shown in Section
3.3.2
that
\vartheta^{h\ell}(0)=0
holds for the choices
\mathcal{P}^{\ell}=\mathcal{P}^{\ell}_{H}
,
\mathcal{P}^{h}=\mathcal{P}^{h}_{H}
and
\mathcal{P}^{\ell}=\mathcal{Q}^{\ell}_{H}
,
\mathcal{P}^{h}=\mathcal{P}^{h}_{V}
. Now we argue as in the proof of Theorem
3.6.6
.
\Box

Proof of Proposition
3.6.9
.
Let
y_{\circ}\in V
,
f\in L^{2}(0,T;H)
and
u\in{\mathscr{U}}
with
\mathcal{B}u\in L^{2}(0,T;H)
. Suppose that Assumptions
3.6.1
and
3.6.7
hold. We set
e^{h\ell}(t)=y^{h}(t)-y^{h\ell}(t)\in V^{h}
for the error between
y^{h}
and
y^{h\ell}
. Then we derive from (
3.6.12
) and (
3.6.16
) that for almost all
t\in[0,T]
and for every
\varphi^{h}\in V^{h}

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\langle e_{t}^{h\ell}(t),\varphi^{h}\rangle}_{V^{\prime},V}+a(e^{h\ell}(t),\varphi^{h})+{\langle\mathcal{C}e^{h\ell}(t),\varphi^{h}\rangle}_{V^{\prime},V}

(3.8.64)

\displaystyle={\langle y_{t}^{h}(t),\varphi^{h}\rangle}_{V^{\prime},V}+a(y^{h}(t),\varphi^{h})+{\langle\mathcal{C}y^{h}(t),\varphi^{h}\rangle}_{V^{\prime},V}

\displaystyle-{\langle y_{t}^{h\ell}(t),\varphi^{h}\rangle}_{V^{\prime},V}-a(y^{h\ell}(t),\varphi^{h})-{\langle\mathcal{C}y^{h\ell}(t),\varphi^{h}\rangle}_{V^{\prime},V}

\displaystyle={\langle\mathcal{N}(y^{h\ell}(t))-\mathcal{N}(y^{h}(t)),\varphi^{h}\rangle}_{V^{\prime},V}-{\langle r^{h\ell}(t),\varphi^{h}\rangle}_{V^{\prime},V}

with the time dependent residual
r^{h\ell}(t)
defined in (
3.6.18
). Choosing
\varphi^{h}=e^{h\ell}(t)\in V^{h}
in (
3.8.64
) we derive from (
3.6.3
) that

\displaystyle{\langle e_{t}^{h\ell}(t),e^{h\ell}(t)\rangle}_{V^{\prime},V}+a(e^{h\ell}(t),e^{h\ell}(t))+{\langle\mathcal{C}e^{h\ell}(t),e^{h\ell}(t)\rangle}_{V^{\prime},V}\geq\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\,{\|e^{h\ell}(t)\|}_{H}^{2}+\gamma_{1}\,{\|e^{h\ell}(t)\|}_{V}^{2}

for almost all
t\in[0,T]
. Recall that the operator
\mathcal{N}
is bilinear. Moreover,
\mathcal{N}(y^{h\ell}(t))=\mathcal{N}(y^{h\ell}(t),y^{h\ell}(t))
and
\mathcal{N}(y^{h}(t))=\mathcal{N}(y^{h}(t),y^{h}(t))
hold for every
t\in[0,T]
. Thus, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}(y^{h\ell}(t))-\mathcal{N}(y^{h}(t))

\displaystyle=\mathcal{M}(y^{h\ell}(t),y^{h\ell}(t))-\mathcal{M}(y^{h}(t),y^{h\ell}(t))

(3.8.65)

\displaystyle-\big(\mathcal{M}(y^{h}(t),y^{h}(t))-\mathcal{M}(y^{h}(t),y^{h\ell}(t))\big)

\displaystyle=-\mathcal{M}(e^{h\ell}(t),y^{h\ell}(t))-\mathcal{M}(y^{h}(t),e^{h\ell}(t))

for almost all
t\in[0,T]
. By (
3.6.5
) we have

\displaystyle{\langle\mathcal{M}(y^{h}(t),e^{h\ell}(t)),e^{h\ell}(t)\rangle}_{V^{\prime},V}=0

for almost all
t\in[0,T]
. Consequently, (
3.8.64
), (
3.6.5
) and the Young inequality imply that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\,{\|e^{h\ell}(t)\|}_{H}^{2}+\gamma_{1}\,{\|e^{h\ell}(t)\|}_{V}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq{\|r^{h\ell}(t)\|}_{(V^{h})^{\prime}}{\|e^{h\ell}(t)\|}_{V}+\big|{\langle\mathcal{M}(e^{h\ell}(t),y^{h\ell}(t)),e^{h\ell}(t)\rangle}_{V^{\prime},V}\big|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{4\gamma_{1}}\,{\|r^{h\ell}(t)\|}_{(V^{h})^{\prime}}^{2}+\frac{\gamma_{1}}{4}\,{\|e^{h\ell}(t)\|}_{V}^{2}+c_{\mathcal{M}}\,{\|e^{h\ell}(t)\|}_{H}{\|y^{h\ell}(t)\|}_{V}{\|e^{h\ell}(t)\|}_{V}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\gamma_{1}}\,{\|r^{h\ell}(t)\|}_{(V^{h})^{\prime}}^{2}+\frac{\gamma_{1}}{2}\,{\|e^{h\ell}(t)\|}_{V}^{2}+\frac{c_{\mathcal{M}}^{2}}{\gamma_{1}}\,{\|y^{h\ell}(t)\|}_{V}^{2}{\|e^{h\ell}(t)\|}_{H}^{2}

which implies

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\,{\|e^{h\ell}(t)\|}_{H}^{2}+\gamma_{1}\,{\|e^{h\ell}(t)\|}_{V}^{2}\leq\frac{2}{\gamma_{1}}\Big({\|r^{h\ell}(t)\|}_{(V^{h})^{\prime}}^{2}+c_{\mathcal{M}}^{2}\,{\|y^{h\ell}(t)\|}_{V}^{2}{\|e^{h\ell}(t)\|}_{H}^{2}\Big)

(3.8.66)

for almost all
t\in[0,T]
. From
e^{h\ell}(0)=y^{h}(0)-y^{h\ell}(0)=(\mathcal{P}^{h}-\mathcal{P}^{h\ell})y_{\circ}
and Proposition
A.1.32
it follows that

{\|e^{h\ell}(t)\|}_{H}^{2}\leq\mathsf{C}_{1}^{h\ell}(t)\bigg(\mathsf{R}_{\circ}^{h\ell}+\int_{0}^{t}\mathsf{R}_{1}^{h\ell}(s)\,\mathrm{d}s\bigg)\quad\text{for }t\in[0,T]\text{ a.e.}

(3.8.67)

with the constants defined in (
3.6.19a
) and (
3.6.19b
). Integrating (
3.8.66
) over
[0,t]
with
t\in(0,T]
and using (
3.8.67
) we find that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}{\|e^{h\ell}(s)\|}_{V}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{\mathsf{R}}_{\circ}^{h\ell}+\int_{0}^{t}\tilde{\mathsf{R}}_{1}^{h\ell}(s)+\frac{2c_{\mathcal{M}}^{2}}{\gamma_{1}^{2}}\,{\|y^{h\ell}(s)\|}_{V}^{2}{\|e^{h\ell}(s)\|}_{H}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{\mathsf{R}}_{\circ}^{h\ell}+\int_{0}^{t}\tilde{\mathsf{R}}_{1}^{h\ell}(s)+\mathsf{R}_{2}^{h\ell}(s)\bigg(\mathsf{R}_{\circ}^{h\ell}+\int_{0}^{s}\mathsf{R}_{1}^{h\ell}(\tau)\,\mathrm{d}\tau\bigg)\,\mathrm{d}s

with the constant in (
3.6.19c
).
\Box

Proof of Proposition
3.6.10
.
We follow the arguments of the proof of Theorem 4.2 in
[
KV02b
]
. Existence of a solution
\{y_{j}^{h}\}_{j=1}^{n}
can be proved by using the Schauder fixed point theorem, see
[
GT77
, p. 222]
, for instance. For that purpose we define
z^{h}=\mathcal{T}_{j}^{h}w^{h}
via the mappings
\mathcal{T}_{j}^{h}:V^{h}\to V^{h}
,
j=1,\ldots,n
, as follows:
z^{h}\in V^{h}
is the solution to

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\langle z^{h},\varphi^{h}\rangle}_{H}+\delta t_{j}\Big(a(z^{h},\varphi^{h})+{\langle\mathcal{C}z^{h}+\mathcal{M}(w^{h},z^{h}),\varphi^{h}\rangle}_{V^{\prime},V}\Big)

(3.8.68)

\displaystyle={\langle y_{j-1}^{h},\varphi^{h}\rangle}_{H}+\delta t_{j}\,{\langle g_{j}(u),\varphi^{h}\rangle}_{V^{\prime},V}\quad\text{for all }\varphi^{h}\in V^{h}.

The bilinear form

\displaystyle{\langle\cdot\,,\cdot\rangle}_{H}+\delta t_{j}\left(a(\cdot\,,\cdot)+{\langle\mathcal{C}(\cdot)+\mathcal{M}(w^{h},\cdot),\cdot\rangle}_{V^{\prime},V}\right)

is continuous and coercive in
V^{h}\times V^{h}
by (
3.6.4
)-(
3.6.5
). The existence and uniqueness of a solution to (
3.8.68
) can thus be shown by the Lax-Milgram theorem. The fixed points of
\mathcal{T}_{j}^{h}
are the solutions of (
3.6.20
). Taking
\varphi^{h}=z^{h}
we infer from (
3.8.68
), (
3.6.3
), (
3.6.5
) and Young’s inequality that

\displaystyle{\|z^{h}\|}_{H}^{2}+\gamma_{1}\delta t_{j}\,{\|z^{h}\|}_{V}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\,{\|y^{h}_{j-1}\|}_{H}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\,{\|z^{h}\|}_{H}^{2}+\frac{\delta t_{j}}{2\gamma_{1}}\,{\|g_{j}(u)\|}_{V^{\prime}}^{2}+\frac{\gamma_{1}\delta t_{j}}{2}\,{\|z^{h}\|}_{V}^{2}

which implies that

\displaystyle{\|z^{h}\|}_{H}^{2}+\gamma_{1}\delta t_{j}\,{\|z^{h}\|}_{V}^{2}\leq{\|y^{h}_{j-1}\|}_{H}^{2}+\frac{\delta t_{j}}{\gamma_{1}}\,{\|g_{j}(u)\|}_{V^{\prime}}^{2}.

Consequently,

{\|z^{h}\|}_{V}\leq\frac{1}{\gamma_{1}^{2}\delta t_{j}}\,\Big(\gamma_{1}{\|y^{h}_{j-1}\|}_{H}^{2}+\delta t_{j}\,{\|g_{j}(u)\|}_{V^{\prime}}^{2}\Big).

(3.8.69)

Let us introduce the set

\displaystyle\mathscr{M}_{j}^{h}=\bigg\{w^{h}\in V^{h}\,\big|\,{\|w^{h}\|}_{V}^{2}\leq\frac{1}{\gamma_{1}^{2}\delta t_{j}}\,\Big(\gamma_{1}{\|y^{h}_{j-1}\|}_{H}^{2}+\delta t_{j}\,{\|g_{j}(u)\|}_{V^{\prime}}^{2}\Big)\bigg\}.

From (
3.8.69
) we infer that
\mathcal{T}_{j}^{h}
maps
\mathscr{M}_{j}^{h}
into itself. Since
\mathscr{M}_{j}^{h}
is a closed ball in
V^{h}
, the set
\mathscr{M}_{j}^{h}
is bounded, closed and convex. Since the image of
\mathcal{T}_{j}^{h}
is finite dimensional,
\mathcal{T}_{j}^{h}
is compact. Thus, the existence of a fixed point
y_{j}^{h}
follows from the Schauder fixed point theorem. Next we show that the solution to (
3.6.20
) is unique. For that purpose we assume that the two sequences
\{y_{j}^{h}\}_{j=1}^{n}
,
\{\tilde{y}_{j}^{h}\}_{j=1}^{n}
in
V^{h}
are solutions of (
3.6.20
). Then
z_{j}^{h}=y_{j}^{h}-\tilde{y}_{j}^{h}\in V^{h}
solves

\displaystyle{\langle z_{j}^{h},\varphi^{h}\rangle}_{H}+\delta t_{j}\left(a(z_{j}^{h},\varphi^{h})+{\langle\mathcal{C}z_{j}^{h},\varphi^{h}\rangle}_{V^{\prime},V}\right)=\delta t_{j}\,{\langle\mathcal{N}(\tilde{y}^{h}_{j})-\mathcal{N}(y_{j}^{h}),\varphi\rangle}_{V^{\prime},V}

for all
\varphi\in V^{h}
. Setting
\varphi=z_{j}^{h}
and using (
3.6.3
), (
3.6.5
) and Young’s inequality we obtain

\displaystyle{\|z_{j}^{h}\|}_{H}^{2}+\gamma_{1}\delta t_{j}\,{\|z_{j}^{h}\|}_{V}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\delta t_{j}\,{\langle\mathcal{N}(\tilde{y}_{j}^{h})-\mathcal{N}(y_{j}^{h}),z_{j}^{k}\rangle}_{V^{\prime},V}=-\delta t_{j}\,{\langle\mathcal{M}(z_{j}^{h},\tilde{y}_{j}^{h})+\mathcal{M}(y_{j}^{h},z_{j}^{h}),z_{j}^{h}\rangle}_{V^{\prime},V}

\displaystyle=-\delta t_{j}~{\langle\mathcal{M}(z_{j}^{h},\tilde{y}_{j}^{h}),z_{j}^{h}\rangle}_{V^{\prime},V}\leq c_{\mathcal{M}}\delta t_{j}\,{\|\tilde{y}_{j}^{h}\|}_{V}{\|z_{j}^{h}\|}_{H}{\|z_{j}^{h}\|}_{V}

\displaystyle\leq{\|z_{j}^{h}\|}_{H}^{2}+\frac{c_{\mathcal{M}}^{2}\delta t_{j}^{2}}{4}~{\|\tilde{y}_{j}^{h}\|}_{V}^{2}{\|z_{j}^{h}\|}_{V}^{2}.

It follows that

\displaystyle\Big(1-\frac{c_{\mathcal{M}}^{2}\delta t_{j}}{4\gamma_{1}}\,{\|\tilde{y}_{j}^{h}\|}_{V}^{2}\Big)\,{\|z_{j}^{h}\|}_{V}^{2}\leq 0.

Let
c=\max\{\|\tilde{y}_{j}^{h}\|_{V}\,|\,j=1,\ldots,n\}
. Then
z_{j}^{h}=0
and hence
y_{j}^{h}=\tilde{y}_{j}^{h}
provided that
\Delta t\leq 4\gamma_{1}/(c^{2}c_{\mathcal{M}}^{2})
.

To prove the estimates (
3.6.23
) and (
3.6.24
) we take
\psi=y_{j}^{h}
in (
3.6.20
). Due to (
3.6.3
), (
3.6.5
) and Lemma
A.2.41
we obtain

\displaystyle{\|y_{j}^{h}\|}_{H}^{2}-{\|y_{j-1}^{h}\|}_{H}^{2}+{\|y_{j}^{h}-y_{j-1}^{h}\|}_{H}^{2}+2\gamma_{1}\delta t_{j}\,{\|y_{j}^{h}\|}_{V}^{2}\leq 2\delta t_{j}~{\|g_{j}(u)\|}_{V^{\prime}}{\|y_{j}^{h}\|}_{V}.

Using Young’s inequality it follows that

{\|y_{j}^{h}\|}_{H}^{2}+{\|y_{j}^{h}-y_{j-1}^{h}\|}_{H}^{2}+\gamma_{1}\delta t_{j}\,{\|y_{j}^{h}\|}_{V}^{2}\leq{\|y_{j-1}^{h}\|}_{H}^{2}+\frac{\delta t_{j}}{\gamma_{1}}\,{\|g_{j}(u)\|}_{V^{\prime}}^{2}.

(3.8.70)

From (
3.8.70
) and (
2.4.1
) we infer that

\displaystyle(1+c_{1}\delta t_{j})\,{\|y_{j}^{h}\|}_{H}^{2}\leq{\|y_{j-1}^{h}\|}_{H}^{2}+\frac{\delta t_{j}}{\gamma_{1}}\,{\|g_{j}(u)\|}_{V^{\prime}}^{2},

where
c_{1}=\gamma_{1}/c_{V}^{2}
, which yields

\displaystyle{\|y_{j}^{h}\|}_{H}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{1+c_{1}\delta t}~{\|y_{j-1}^{h}\|}_{H}^{2}+\frac{\delta t_{j}}{\gamma_{1}(1+c_{1}\delta t_{j})}\,{\|g_{j}(u)\|}_{V^{\prime}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\bigg(\frac{1}{1+c_{1}\delta t}\bigg)^{j-1}\bigg({\|y_{1}^{h}\|}_{H}^{2}+\frac{1}{\gamma_{1}}\sum_{l=2}^{j}\delta t_{l}\,{\|g_{l}(u)\|}_{V^{\prime}}^{2}\bigg)

which coincides (
3.8.31
). Hence we can proceed as in the proof of Theorem
3.5.2
. Suppose that
\delta t\leq 1/C_{1}
holds with
C_{1}=2c_{1}
(cf. (
3.8.32
)). Then we get directly (
3.6.23
) for
j=2,\ldots,n
. Summation upon
j
in (
3.8.70
) we obtain (
3.6.24
).
\Box

Proof of Theorem
3.6.13
.
Again we utilize the decomposition

\displaystyle y_{j}^{h}-y_{j}^{h\ell}=y_{j}^{h}-\mathcal{P}^{n\ell}y_{j}^{h}+\mathcal{P}^{n\ell}y_{j}^{h}-y_{j}^{h\ell}=\varrho_{j}^{h\ell}+\vartheta_{j}^{h\ell}\quad\text{for }1\leq j\leq n

with
\varrho_{j}^{h\ell}=y_{j}^{h}-\mathcal{P}^{n\ell}y_{j}^{h}\in(X^{n\ell})^{\bot}
and
\vartheta_{j}^{h\ell}=\mathcal{P}^{n\ell}y_{j}^{h}-y_{j}^{h\ell}\in X^{n\ell}
. Here, we consider the
H
-orthonormal projections. Thus, we take
\mathcal{P}^{n\ell}=\mathcal{P}^{n\ell}_{H}
for
X=H
and
\mathcal{P}^{n\ell}=\mathcal{Q}^{h\ell}_{H}
for
X=V
. It follows from Proposition
2.4.17
that

\sum_{j=1}^{n}\alpha_{j}^{n}\,{\|\varrho_{j}^{h\ell}\|}^{2}_{V}=\sum_{j=1}^{n}\alpha_{j}^{n}\,{\|y_{j}^{h}-\mathcal{P}_{H}^{n\ell}y_{j}^{h}\|}^{2}_{V}=\sum_{i=\ell+1}^{d^{n}}\lambda_{i}^{nH}\,{\|\psi_{i}^{nH}\|}_{V}^{2}

(3.8.71)

for
X=H
and

\sum_{j=1}^{n}\alpha_{j}^{n}\,{\|\varrho_{j}^{h\ell}\|}^{2}_{V}=\sum_{j=1}^{n}\alpha_{j}^{n}\,{\|y_{j}^{h}-\mathcal{Q}_{H}^{n\ell}y_{j}^{h}\|}^{2}_{V}=\sum_{i=\ell+1}^{d^{n}}\lambda_{i}^{nV}\,{\|\psi_{i}^{nV}-\mathcal{P}^{n\ell}\psi_{i}^{nV}\|}_{V}^{2}

(3.8.72)

for
X=V
. Now we follow Lemma 4.5 in
[
KV02b
]
. Since
y_{1}^{h}=\mathcal{P}^{h}_{H}y_{\circ}
holds, we have
\vartheta_{1}^{h\ell}=0
for our chosen projections
\mathcal{P}^{n\ell}
. Using the notaion
\overline{\partial}\vartheta_{j}^{h\ell}=(\vartheta_{j}^{h\ell}-\vartheta_{j-1}^{h\ell})/\delta t_{j}
,
j=2,\ldots,n
, we obtain for every
\psi\in X^{n\ell}

\displaystyle{\langle\overline{\partial}\vartheta_{j}^{h\ell},\psi\rangle}_{H}+a(\vartheta_{j}^{h\ell},\psi)+{\langle\mathcal{C}\vartheta_{j}^{h\ell},\psi\rangle}_{V^{\prime},V}

\displaystyle={\langle\overline{\partial}\mathcal{P}^{n\ell}y_{j}^{h},\psi\rangle}_{H}+a(\mathcal{P}^{n\ell}y_{j}^{h},\psi)+{\langle\mathcal{C}\mathcal{P}^{n\ell}y_{j}^{h},\psi\rangle}_{V^{\prime},V}

(3.8.73)

\displaystyle-\big({\langle\overline{\partial}y_{j}^{h\ell},\psi\rangle}_{H}+a(y_{j}^{h\ell},\psi)+{\langle\mathcal{C}y_{j}^{h\ell},\psi\rangle}_{V^{\prime},V}\big).

From (
3.8.44
) it follows that

\displaystyle{\langle\overline{\partial}\mathcal{P}^{n\ell}y_{j}^{h},\psi^{h}\rangle}_{H}={\langle\overline{\partial}y_{j}^{h},\psi\rangle}_{H}\quad\text{for }j=2,\ldots,n.

Thus, we infer from (
3.8.73
), (
3.6.20
) and (
3.6.29
) that

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\langle\overline{\partial}\vartheta_{j}^{h\ell},\psi\rangle}_{H}+a(\vartheta_{j}^{h\ell},\psi)+{\langle\vartheta_{j}^{h\ell},\psi\rangle}_{V^{\prime},V}

(3.8.74)
